Cross-model extraction (Mistral-7B extracts constraints from Qwen2.5-14B traces) 完全无效：SICA = SC = 75.00%, discriminative_rate=0%, recovery_rate=0%. Mistral 虽然是不同模型家族，但提取的 constraints 同样缺乏判别力。这进一步强化 capability trap 结论：问题不在 same-model bias，而在 LLM extraction 本身的能力限制——无论哪个模型做 extraction，都无法从 CoT traces 中提取出对答案有判别力的 symbolic constraints。论文 claim: Oracle-SICA (gold FOL + Z3 consistency check + MAX-SAT) 88.24% vs SC 79.41% vs SICA(self-extracted) 79.41%, +8.82pp, McNemar p=6.15e-05 高度显著。Z3 solver 与 gold answer 一致率 94.6%（193/204），FOL parser 204/204 全部成功。SICA(self)=SC 确认 LLM 自提取约束在 formal logic 上无有效信号。结论：MAX-SAT 聚合机制本身有效——瓶颈在约束提取质量，不在聚合算法。这直接回答了 Reviewer 的核心 confound 质疑。\\\"}, \\\"temperature-ablation-t05\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"notes\\\": \\\"SICA overhead 分析 (804题)：总时间 61.6s/题，其中 trace 生成 31.6s (51.3%)，约束提取 29.0s (47.0%)，去重 1.0s (1.6%)，MaxSAT 0.003s (≈0%)。SICA vs SC 多花 30.0s/题 (+48.7%)，开销几乎全部来自 constraint extraction LLM 只保留在≥T条traces中重复出现的约束能否提升SICA accuracy超过SC——验证约束质量过滤是否解决capability trap\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"sica_expanded_14b\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\", \\\"notes\\\": \\\"NEGATIVE confirmed. T=1/2/3 结果完全一致(+2.33pp=1题翻转), T≥5 SICA退化为SC。Discriminative analysis: unique constraints 中 11.5% 是 discriminative (by count), 37.2% (by weight)。但有效 flip 率仅 4/23=17.4%。根本原因：提取的约束是 domain facts (kind_charlie==True, Implies(...)) — 无论答案是什么，中间推理步骤大部分共通。约束系统未提取答案级别判别谓词。Constraint filtering 天花板极低，不是 rescue path。\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"n\\\": 43, \\\"thresholds_tested\\\": [1, 2, 3, 5, 7], \"2026-05-18T17:18:15.484Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Z3 UNSAT core feedback refinement: closed-loop 方法用 Z3 solver 的 UNSAT core 指导 LLM 修正矛盾约束（最多 3 轮）。如果约束质量是瓶颈，solver 反馈应能提升 constraint discriminative power，使 Z3-feedback SICA > vanilla SICA。目标 recovery_rate > 0%（recovery_rate = Z3fb_delta / oracle_delta × 100%）。与之前 open-loop interventions（contrastive/cross-model/generational 全部失败）对比，closed-loop 利用 formal verification 信号可能是打破 capability trap 的关键。\"}, \"exp-007-qwen72b-awq\": {\"status\": \"RUNNING\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-18T16:16:47.099Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-18T16:58:52.128Z\", <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QrdTHpy272ST8tzx5HPPWZ`</summary>

```text
103	\subsection{Scope and Limitations}
104	\label{sec:scope}
105	
106	SICA as evaluated here uses \emph{same-model self-extraction}: the same LLM generates traces and extracts constraints. This isolates whether an LLM's own reasoning can be usefully formalized for aggregation. Published methods achieving positive results through different mechanisms \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} are complementary to our findings, as each introduces capabilities beyond the reasoning model itself. An oracle variant (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) disentangles extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness. Beyond evaluating the standard pipeline, we test two targeted interventions that probe specific hypothesized causes of the capability trap: contrastive extraction, which forces the model to generate answer-discriminative constraints, and cross-model extraction, which breaks the self-reference loop by using a separate model for constraint extraction (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}).
107	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0165LBaZwyfbP9KwaQi6SqWd`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We evaluated Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters, including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains with more than 1,500 total instances. Self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over Self-Consistency in any tested condition, with accuracy gaps ranging from $-3.3$ to $+3.3$\,pp. Oracle experiments replacing self-extraction with gold annotations yield $+8.82$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$). This result causally localizes the failure to extraction quality: the MAX-SAT aggregation mechanism is sound when supplied with high-quality constraints, but same-model self-extraction cannot produce them. We characterize this as a \emph{capability trap}: because the reasoning model also extracts the constraints, those constraints inherit the same biases that produced potentially flawed answers and carry no independent discriminative signal. The failure is not one of aggregation design but of the circular dependency between generation and verification within a single model.
4	
5	The practical consequences follow from the constraint quality analysis (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}, \S\ref{sec:ablation}). Over 91\% of self-extracted constraints are semantically non-discriminative (8.2\% Z3 semantic discriminative rate, $N = 804$), which reduces MAX-SAT to a weighted vote count (803/804 agreement with count-only). The extraction step roughly doubles wall-clock cost for zero expected gain. For practitioners, the multi-candidate rate $C_0$ provides a zero-cost screening criterion computable from trace samples alone: low $C_0$ rules out any SICA benefit by construction, while high $C_0$ warrants a constraint quality audit before committing to full-scale extraction.
6	
7	The oracle gaps ($+8.82$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, combined $N = 804$) quantify recoverable potential in formal logic domains; extension to commonsense and science domains requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. Trained extractors, cross-model pipelines, and external provers each target the extraction bottleneck directly, as discussed in \S\ref{sec:discussion}.
```

</details>

### Tool result

_source line 2343 · 2026-05-18T17:27:35.405Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Maa7TpAagLKMdfWAHVdmzE`</summary>

```text
8	The computational cost is substantial: constraint extraction accounts for 48.7\% of total SICA pipeline time (measured on Qwen2.5-14B, FOLIO + ProofWriter combined, $n = 804$, single A6000 GPU), roughly doubling wall-clock cost compared to SC alone. SICA and SC disagree on 31 of 804 problems (3.9\%), but these disagreements approximately cancel: 16 SICA wins, 14 SC wins, and 1 both-wrong with different answers, yielding a net gain of only $+$2 problems (Appendix~\ref{app:case-studies} provides representative case studies). Bootstrap confidence intervals bound the true effect to [$-1.12$, $+1.62$]\,pp for the largest confirmatory condition ($n = 804$; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:bootstrap}): even if a small effect exists, it cannot justify the pipeline's additional complexity and cost.
9	
10	The oracle ceilings ($+8.82$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter) quantify the potential locked behind the capability trap, with larger gains on harder relational reasoning subtypes (RelNeg $+17.86$\,pp). The oracle gap suggests that constraint quality, not the extraction prompt format, is the primary bottleneck, though alternative extraction strategies (e.g., few-shot examples, iterative refinement, structured decomposition) were not tested in this study. Three concrete paths can unlock the potential. First, trained FOL extractors supervised on datasets with gold annotations (such as FOLIO) can learn extraction patterns that exceed same-model capabilities; FoVer \citep{wang2025fover} demonstrates this approach with a dedicated verifier. Second, external provers that verify constraints independently of the reasoning model eliminate the circular dependency between generation and verification. Third, cross-model extraction, where a separate model extracts constraints from another model's traces, breaks the self-reference loop that produces non-discriminative constraints. Each path targets the extraction bottleneck directly; since MAX-SAT and count-only produce identical predictions on 803/804 instances, improving the aggregation mechanism offers no path forward.
11	
12	For practitioners considering constraint-based voting, $C_0$ provides a zero-cost screening criterion computable from the trace sampling stage alone, before any constraint extraction or solver invocation. When $C_0$ falls below 5\%, SICA degenerates to SC by construction. High $C_0$ is necessary but not sufficient, as the capability trap renders constraints non-discriminative even when disagreement is abundant ($C_0 = 61.8\%$, $\Delta = -2.45$~pp, n.s., for LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO). The recommended workflow is: compute $C_0$, and if it exceeds 5\%, assess constraint quality on a small pilot before committing to full-scale extraction. More broadly, the evaluation framework developed here (oracle component isolation, dual discriminativeness metrics, and $C_0$ screening) applies to any pipeline where an LLM extracts formal structure from its own outputs. This reusable methodology can diagnose self-extraction bottlenecks beyond the SICA setting.
13	
14	\paragraph{Implications for Test-Time Scaling.}
15	Scaling test-time computation through additional reasoning traces faces diminishing returns under majority voting \citep{snell2025scaling, loo2025reevaluating}. Our oracle results demonstrate that symbolic verification can break this plateau: gold FOL constraints yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, extracting substantial signal from the same traces that majority voting cannot use. The bottleneck is not the verification mechanism but the constraint source. Self-extracted constraints inherit the correlated errors of the traces they were derived from, producing what \citet{kambhampati2024position} term ``fake verification'' where the verifier cannot catch errors the generator makes. Z3 Feedback Refinement (\S\ref{sec:z3-feedback}) represents one attempt to break this circularity through the LLM-Modulo paradigm, where an external solver provides corrective signals that the model cannot generate through introspection. Whether this partial external grounding suffices to improve constraint discriminativeness remains an open empirical question (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}). For practitioners, $C_0$ offers a zero-cost signal for whether test-time scaling has reached the majority-voting ceiling: when $C_0 < 5\%$, additional traces provide no new information, and investing in verification infrastructure rather than more samples becomes the rational allocation of test-time compute.
```

</details>

```json
  "sessionId": "6e0077cd-ef03-4ff5-b72c-7cf0ad14c0f1",
  "content": "[Director 指令] [Reframe — URGENT] 论文核心 framing 重大变化（D052）\n\n**变化**：从 \"universal capability trap (SICA never works)\" 改为 \"regime-dependent capability trap (SICA's effectiveness depends on model accuracy)\"。\n\n**触发证据**：exp-033 Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3 on FOLIO-204 显示 SICA +4.90pp (p=0.013, statistically significant)，SC=54.41%。这是首个显著正结果。与 Qwen-14B (SC=75.49%, Δ=+0.25pp n.s.) 对比，构成准确率依赖的 regime 边界。\n\n**新 narrative**：\n- 高准确率模型 (SC ≥ ~70%): self-extracted constraints 与模型答案高度相关 (confirmation bias) → non-discriminative → trap active → SICA ≈ SC\n- 低准确率模型 (SC ≤ ~55%): constraints 保留部分独立性 → SICA can help (+4.90pp) → 但仍远低于 oracle ceiling (+14.22pp)\n- Oracle 仍然是核心贡献（证明 aggregation mechanism 有效）\n- 6 remediation strategies 仍全部失败（证明 extraction quality 是 bottleneck）\n- C0 升级为二维 diagnostic: C0 + SC accuracy\n\n**受影响章节**：Abstract, Introduction (贡献列表), §4 Results (main table 需新增 Mistral 行 + regime analysis), §5 Analysis/Discussion (regime boundary characterization), Conclusion。Method (§3) 不变。\n\n**现在的行动**：\n1. **暂停**任何基于 \"SICA never works\" framing 的写作\n2. 等我确认 Qwen-7B SC 准确率（决定 regime boundary 是否仅 Mistral-specific）\n3. 更新后的 paper_framing.md 已就绪，请重新读取\n\n**预计 2-3 小时后**会有更多数据（PW-600 full results, constructive experiments, westd-16639 实验结果），届时发完整 Reframe 指令。这次不用验证引用。"
}
```

[Director 指令] [Reframe — URGENT] 论文核心 framing 重大变化（D052）

**变化**：从 "universal capability trap (SICA never works)" 改为 "regime-dependent capability trap (SICA's effectiveness depends on model accuracy)"。

**触发证据**：exp-033 Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3 on FOLIO-204 显示 SICA +4.90pp (p=0.013, statistically significant)，SC=54.41%。这是首个显著正结果。与 Qwen-14B (SC=75.49%, Δ=+0.25pp n.s.) 对比，构成准确率依赖的 regime 边界。

**新 narrative**：
- 高准确率模型 (SC ≥ ~70%): self-extracted constraints 与模型答案高度相关 (confirmation bias) → non-discriminative → trap active → SICA ≈ SC
- 低准确率模型 (SC ≤ ~55%): constraints 保留部分独立性 → SICA can help (+4.90pp) → 但仍远低于 oracle ceiling (+14.22pp)
- Oracle 仍然是核心贡献（证明 aggregation mechanism 有效）
- 6 remediation strategies 仍全部失败（证明 extraction quality 是 bottleneck）
- C0 升级为二维 diagnostic: C0 + SC accuracy

**受影响章节**：Abstract, Introduction (贡献列表), §4 Results (main table 需新增 Mistral 行 + regime analysis), §5 Analysis/Discussion (regime boundary characterization), Conclusion。Method (§3) 不变。

**现在的行动**：
1. **暂停**任何基于 "SICA never works" framing 的写作
2. 等我确认 Qwen-7B SC 准确率（决定 regime boundary 是否仅 Mistral-specific）
3. 更新后的 paper_framing.md 已就绪，请重新读取

**预计 2-3 小时后**会有更多数据（PW-600 full results, constructive experiments, westd-16639 实验结果），届时发完整 Reframe 指令。这次不用验证引用。

The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap of Self-Extracted Constraints\"\n\n## Core Narrative (详见 paper_framing.md)\nLLM self-extracted FOL constraints 的判别力取决于模型自身准确率：\n- **High-SC regime (≥~60%)**: Confirmation bias → constraints 退化为 counting → SICA≈SC\n- **Low-SC regime (<~55%)**: Constraints 保留部分独立性 → SICA 显著提升 (Mistral +4.90pp, p=0.013)\n- **Sharp boundary**: Within-model ICL transition 证明 SC accuracy 是因果机制（同一 Mistral-7B，SC 54%→61.5%，SICA 从 +4.90pp 翻转为 -2.23pp）\n- **Oracle ceiling**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT = +14.22pp (p=2e-7)，Mistral 仅恢复 ~35%\n- **六个 remediation 全部失败**: Contrastive prompting, cross-model, generational scaling, Z3 feedback, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT\n\n## 4 Core Claims\n1. Oracle proves aggregation mechanism, localizes bottleneck to extraction\n\n## 关键数据点（论文必须包含）\n| Model | Dataset | SC% | SICA Δpp | p-value |\n|-------|---------|-----|----------|---------|\n| Oracle (gold FOL) | FOLIO-204 | 75.49 | **+14.22** | 2.0e-7 |\n| **Mistral-7B** | FOLIO-204 | 54.41 | **+4.90** | **0.013** |\n| Mistral-7B+ICL | FOLIO-204 | 61.5 | -2.23 | partial |\n| Qwen2.5-14B | FOLIO-204 | 75.49 | +0.25 | n.s. |\n| Qwen2.5-14B | LogiQA-200 | 78.5 | -0.50 | n.s. |\n| Qwen2.5-7B | FOLIO-204 | 77.34 | -0.49 | n.s. |\n| Qwen3-14B | FOLIO-204 | 83.82 | +0.49 | 0.655 |\n| LLaMA-8B | FOLIO-204 | 63.24 | -2.45 | 0.267 |\n| LLaMA-8B | StrategyQA-200 | — | +2.0 | n.s. |\n| DeepSeek-R1-7B | FOLIO-60 | — | -3.3 | n.s. |\n| QwQ-32B | multi | — | 0 | C0<5% |\n\n## Paper Structure\n- T1: Main results table (models × domains, highlight regime boundary)\n- T2: Oracle control (Gold FOL vs SC vs SICA-self)\n- T3: Constraint ablation (MaxSAT ≈ count-only ≈ random)\n- T4: 6 remediation failures\n- F1: Method pipeline\n- F2: **Hero figure** — SC% vs SICA Δ scatter (regime boundary visualization)\n- F3: Per-problem constraint precision (Mistral vs Qwen comparison)\n- F4: C0 × SC diagnostic heatmap\n\n## 待补数据（Paper Agent 先用 placeholder，后续 Patch）\n- exp-034/039 PW-600 全量结果（正在运行/分析中）\n- exp-039/040 PW-600 (Qwen2.5-14B / Qwen3 on 16639)\n- Constraint precision comparison 分析（Worker 已完成，等 Agent 汇报）\n\n## 引用需要验证\npaper_framing.md \"Required Citations\" 列出了必须引用的类别和具体论文。\n\n## 注意事项\n- 论文定位是 analysis paper with both positive and negative results，不是 method paper\n- 必须在 title 和 abstract 中明确 scope: \"self-extracted constraints\" / \"SICA\"，不是 \"symbolic reasoning\" broadly\n- Published methods (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer) achieve gains on same benchmarks using different approaches — acknowledge in Related Work\n- n=60 conditions 用 \"suggestive/consistent with\"，n≥200 才能 \"confirm\"\n- 严格遵守 paper-writing-norms（无 dash、无 -ing phrases、无 AI style markers）"

_source line 2371 · 2026-05-18T20:22:53.264Z_

[Director 指令] [Reframe] 论文 framing 已从 "universal capability trap" 升级为 "regime-dependent capability trap" (D052/D055)。这是一次完整重写，影响所有章节。

## 投稿目标
EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25（~7 天）。8pp + appendix。

## 新 Working Title
"When Does Symbolic Aggregation Help? The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap of Self-Extracted Constraints"

## Core Narrative (详见 paper_framing.md)
LLM self-extracted FOL constraints 的判别力取决于模型自身准确率：
- **High-SC regime (≥~60%)**: Confirmation bias → constraints 退化为 counting → SICA≈SC
- **Low-SC regime (<~55%)**: Constraints 保留部分独立性 → SICA 显著提升 (Mistral +4.90pp, p=0.013)
- **Sharp boundary**: Within-model ICL transition 证明 SC accuracy 是因果机制（同一 Mistral-7B，SC 54%→61.5%，SICA 从 +4.90pp 翻转为 -2.23pp）
- **Oracle ceiling**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT = +14.22pp (p=2e-7)，Mistral 仅恢复 ~35%
- **六个 remediation 全部失败**: Contrastive prompting, cross-model, generational scaling, Z3 feedback, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT

## 4 Core Claims
1. Regime-dependent capability trap（SC accuracy 决定 SICA 效果）
2. Regime boundary characterization（sharp threshold at ~55% SC，within-model evidence）
3. C0 + SC two-dimensional deployment diagnostic
4. Oracle proves aggregation mechanism, localizes bottleneck to extraction

## 关键数据点（论文必须包含）
| Model | Dataset | SC% | SICA Δpp | p-value |
|-------|---------|-----|----------|---------|
| Oracle (gold FOL) | FOLIO-204 | 75.49 | **+14.22** | 2.0e-7 |
| **Mistral-7B** | FOLIO-204 | 54.41 | **+4.90** | **0.013** |
| Mistral-7B+ICL | FOLIO-204 | 61.5 | -2.23 | partial |
| Qwen2.5-14B | FOLIO-204 | 75.49 | +0.25 | n.s. |
| Qwen2.5-14B | LogiQA-200 | 78.5 | -0.50 | n.s. |
| Qwen2.5-7B | FOLIO-204 | 77.34 | -0.49 | n.s. |
| Qwen3-14B | FOLIO-204 | 83.82 | +0.49 | 0.655 |
| LLaMA-8B | FOLIO-204 | 63.24 | -2.45 | 0.267 |
| LLaMA-8B | StrategyQA-200 | — | +2.0 | n.s. |
| DeepSeek-R1-7B | FOLIO-60 | — | -3.3 | n.s. |
| QwQ-32B | multi | — | 0 | C0<5% |

## Paper Structure
- T1: Main results table (models × domains, highlight regime boundary)
- T2: Oracle control (Gold FOL vs SC vs SICA-self)
- T3: Constraint ablation (MaxSAT ≈ count-only ≈ random)
- T4: 6 remediation failures
- F1: Method pipeline
- F2: **Hero figure** — SC% vs SICA Δ scatter (regime boundary visualization)
- F3: Per-problem constraint precision (Mistral vs Qwen comparison)
- F4: C0 × SC diagnostic heatmap

## 待补数据（Paper Agent 先用 placeholder，后续 Patch）
- exp-034/039 PW-600 全量结果（正在运行/分析中）
- exp-039/040 PW-600 (Qwen2.5-14B / Qwen3 on 16639)
- Constraint precision comparison 分析（Worker 已完成，等 Agent 汇报）

## 引用需要验证
paper_framing.md "Required Citations" 列出了必须引用的类别和具体论文。

## 注意事项
- 论文定位是 analysis paper with both positive and negative results，不是 method paper
- 必须在 title 和 abstract 中明确 scope: "self-extracted constraints" / "SICA"，不是 "symbolic reasoning" broadly
- Published methods (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer) achieve gains on same benchmarks using different approaches — acknowledge in Related Work
- n=60 conditions 用 "suggestive/consistent with"，n≥200 才能 "confirm"
- 严格遵守 paper-writing-norms（无 dash、无 -ing phrases、无 AI style markers）

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016tYD2Sg6pg8obt7XHpA2h5`</summary>

```text
1	# Paper Framing — symb_invariant_consensus
2	
3	## Target Venue
4	- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25)
5	- Deadline: May 25, 2026 (ARR submission)
6	
7	## Core Claims (4 条) — D052 Regime-Dependent Capability Trap (REVISED)
8	
9	1. **The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap**: LLM self-extracted FOL constraint aggregation faces a regime-dependent tension. When model accuracy is high (SC ≥ ~70%), self-extracted constraints correlate strongly with the model's own answers (confirmation bias), losing discriminative power and reducing MAX-SAT to counting. When model accuracy is low (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, enabling SICA to provide genuine gains. The trap is not universal but accuracy-conditioned. Validated across 3 reasoning domains × 5 model families spanning instruction-scale, distilled-reasoning, and full-reasoning architectures. [Scope: this finding characterizes same-model self-extraction pipelines; methods using external verifiers or trained extractors may not share this limitation.]
10	2. **Regime Boundary — SC Accuracy Determines SICA Effectiveness**: (a) **High-accuracy regime (trap active)**: Qwen-14B SC=75.49% Δ=+0.25pp n.s. (n=804), Qwen-14B LogiQA SC=78.8% Δ=0.0pp (n=151 interim), LLaMA-8B StrategyQA Δ=+2.0pp n.s. (n=200), LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204), DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B Δ=-3.3pp (n=60). Full reasoning model QwQ-32B C0<5% (degenerate). (b) **Low-accuracy regime (trap partially lifted)**: Mistral-7B SC=54.41% Δ=**+4.90pp p=0.013** (n=204, b=12 c=2; McNemar confirmed by programmatic computation, three manual attempts all had transcription errors). BUT: LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204) — need LLaMA-8B SC to determine if this is also low-SC (which would complicate the simple accuracy-threshold story) or medium-SC. The boundary may involve more than just SC accuracy (model architecture, constraint extraction quality, variable naming consistency).
11	3. **C0 as Necessary-but-Insufficient Diagnostic**: C0 reliably predicts when symbolic consensus CANNOT help (C0<5% → method degenerates to SC), but high C0 does NOT guarantee gains. C0 combined with SC accuracy forms a two-dimensional screening: low C0 → degenerate; high C0 + high SC → trap; high C0 + low SC → potential gains. Zero-cost early stopping criterion for practitioners.
12	4. **Oracle Proves Aggregation Mechanism, Localizes Bottleneck**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT achieves +14.22pp over SC (89.71% vs 75.49%, p=2.0e-7), proving the aggregation paradigm IS effective with discriminative constraints. Even Mistral-7B's significant +4.90pp captures only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality remains the binding bottleneck across all regimes. Six remediation strategies all failed to close this gap (contrastive prompting, cross-model extraction, generational scaling, solver-guided refinement, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT).
13	
14	## Core Insight
15	LLM self-extracted FOL constraints exhibit a **regime-dependent capability trap**: their discriminative power depends on the extracting model's own accuracy on the task. When a model is strong (SC ≥ ~70%), its self-extracted constraints are strongly correlated with its own answers (confirmation bias), losing the independence needed to adjudicate between candidates. MAX-SAT over such constraints degenerates to counting. When a model is weak (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, and SICA can provide significant gains (Mistral-7B: +4.90pp, p=0.013, b=12 c=2). However, even in the favorable regime, self-extracted constraints capture only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality as the persistent bottleneck.
16	
17	**Key evidence**:
18	- **Oracle control (exp-031)**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown-type questions +31.9pp. Proves aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints.
19	- **Regime boundary**: Qwen-14B (SC=75.49%) Δ=+0.25pp n.s. vs Mistral-7B (SC=54.41%) Δ=+4.90pp p=0.013. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%) Δ=-2.45pp shows the boundary is sharp (not all weak models benefit).
20	- **Within-model regime transition (D053)**: ICL Oracle on Mistral-7B (exp-044 partial n=148) boosted SC from 54.4%→61.5% via oracle examples, but SICA flipped from +4.90pp to -2.23pp. Same model, same architecture, only SC accuracy changed. This controls for model-specific factors and directly demonstrates the regime transition mechanism.
21	- **Six remediation failures**: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model extraction (exp-030, 0.00pp), generational scaling (exp-027, +0.49pp n.s.), solver-guided refinement (exp-036, 0.9% UNSAT trigger rate), consistency filtering (constraints too sparse), cross-trace UNSAT (56.4% zero shared variables). All failed to close the extraction-oracle gap, confirming the trap is structural across prompting, architecture, capability, and verification dimensions.
22	
23	We provide the first systematic empirical study (a) identifying the regime boundary where self-extracted constraint aggregation transitions from ineffective to beneficial, (b) localizing the failure mode to extraction quality via oracle control, (c) quantifying constraint non-discrimination at per-problem granularity, and (d) proposing C0 + SC accuracy as a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic. [Positioning: Logic-LM and LINC documented that LLM-to-FOL translation is error-prone; our contribution is the oracle-controlled causal separation of extraction vs aggregation failure, the regime boundary characterization, and systematic quantification across 5 model families.]
24	
25	## Story Spine
26	- **Problem**: Recent work proposes augmenting LLM reasoning with symbolic methods, extracting formal constraints from reasoning traces and using solvers for answer selection. But when should practitioners invest in such methods vs. simple majority voting?
27	- **Existing approaches & gaps**: SC (Wang et al. 2023), USC (Chen et al. 2023), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023), ORM/PRM (Cobbe et al. 2021, Lightman et al. 2023), Weighted SC (Taubenfeld et al. 2024). None systematically characterize when symbolic aggregation helps vs. when it degenerates to voting.
28	- **Our framing**: We instantiate symbolic consensus as SICA (MAX-SAT over extracted FOL constraints) and systematically evaluate across 3 domains × 5 model families + 1 full reasoning model. We discover a regime-dependent capability trap: SICA gains are accuracy-conditioned, not universally absent.
29	- **Headline result**: "Across 5 model families, SICA's effectiveness depends on the extracting model's baseline accuracy. Strong models (SC ≥ 70%) show no gain (Δ = -3.3pp to +2.0pp, all n.s.); a weaker model (Mistral-7B, SC = 54%) shows significant improvement (+4.90pp, p = 0.013). Oracle control (+14.22pp) proves the aggregation mechanism works with discriminative constraints, but even the best self-extraction result captures only ~35% of this ceiling. Six remediation strategies all fail to close the gap. We identify a confirmation-bias mechanism: stronger models' constraints mirror their own answers, losing discriminative power. C0 + SC accuracy form a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic."
30	
31	## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Defensive Experiments
32	1. **"Gains not statistically significant"** → Four powered tests: exp-001 (n=804), exp-019 (n=200), exp-020 (n=204), exp-010 (n=60). Combined N>1,200.
33	2. **"Only a better tiebreaker, not worth the overhead"** → exp-002: K ablation (SICA≤SC all K including odd K=11); exp-013: MaxSAT≈count-only (mechanism vacuous); exp-009: overhead analysis
34	3. **"Only Qwen models"** → 5 model families: Qwen-14B + Mistral-7B + LLaMA-8B + DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B + QwQ-32B. Critically, Mistral-7B shows the ONLY significant positive (+4.90pp, p=0.013), strengthening cross-family coverage AND revealing the regime boundary.
35	4. **"Only one reasoning model"** → QwQ-32B (full reasoning, C0<5%). DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B reclassified as instruction-scale (C0=58%, behaves like instruction model due to 7B capacity insufficient for deterministic reasoning chains)
36	5. **"Only logic domain"** → FOLIO (formal logic) + StrategyQA (commonsense) + ARC-Challenge (science). ProofWriter in appendix.
37	6. **"MAX-SAT is over-engineering"** → exp-013: MaxSAT ≈ count-only ≈ random-weight confirmed. This IS the capability trap evidence.
38	7. **"Random tiebreak baseline"** → exp-003: SICA tiebreak 80% vs random 50% vs alphabetical 60%. On tied questions SICA is better, but ties are too rare to matter at scale.
39	8. **"Why not just use a reasoning model?"** → Full reasoning model QwQ-32B shows C0<5%, SICA redundant. Distilled reasoning model DeepSeek-R1-7B behaves like instruction model (C0=58%). Either way SICA provides no benefit.
40	9. **"How does this compare to Logic-LM / LINC?"** → exp-015: Logic-LM × K baseline (CPU-only)
41	10. **"Weighted SC is simpler"** → exp-016: Weighted SC baseline (CPU-only)
42	11. **"C0 is trivially derived"** → C0 is trivial to compute but non-trivial to interpret. Its value is as a zero-cost early stopping criterion, not as a metric per se.
43	12. **"Within-family scaling?"** → exp-004 (7B) + exp-007 (72B-AWQ) if time permits. C0 trend within Qwen family.
44	13. **"Self-extraction confound — same model generates and extracts"** → exp-031 Oracle FOLIO (post-fix traces): **RESOLVED.** Gold FOL + Z3 + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC = 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown +31.9pp. Aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints. Bottleneck conclusively localized to LLM self-extraction quality. Three remediation attempts all failed: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model Mistral→Qwen (exp-030, 0pp), Qwen3 generational (exp-027, +0.49pp p=0.655 n.s.). This is now a core contribution, not just a defensive experiment.
45	14. **"Published methods achieve gains on same benchmarks (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer)"** → Acknowledge in Related Work and Discussion. Key distinction: those methods use trained/external verifiers, multi-paradigm solver selection, or logic-complete decomposition — all fundamentally different from same-model self-extraction. Our finding characterizes the specific failure mode of self-extraction pipelines, not symbolic reasoning broadly.
46	15. **"MAX-SAT formulation is just weighted voting"** → Paper must explicitly describe cross-answer evaluation: constraints from trace t_i ARE/ARE NOT evaluated against other candidates. If structurally a weighted vote, acknowledge and frame contribution as "even the most charitable interpretation (weighted vote with constraint-based weights) provides no gain."
47	
48	## Working Title
49	**"When Does Symbolic Aggregation Help? The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap of Self-Extracted Constraints"**
50	(Alternatives: 
51	- "The Capability Trap: Self-Extracted Constraints Help Weak Models but Not Strong Ones"
52	- "Diversity Without Discrimination: A Systematic Study of When LLM-Extracted Constraint Aggregation Succeeds and Fails")
53	
54	NOTE (Dream D037): Title MUST be scoped to "self-extracted constraints" / "SICA" — NOT "symbolic consensus" broadly. Published methods (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer, LogicAgent) achieve gains on same benchmarks using different symbolic approaches. Our evidence covers one pipeline (same-model FOL extraction + MAX-SAT), not the entire paradigm.
55	
56	## Paper Structure (EMNLP 8pp + appendix)
57	- Main Tables: T1 (Main Results: models × domains, SICA vs SC, highlighting regime boundary), T2 (Oracle Control: Gold-FOL+MAX-SAT vs SC vs SICA-self, causal separation), T3 (Constraint Ablation: MaxSAT vs count-only vs random for high-SC model), T4 (Remediation Attempts: 6 interventions all failed to close oracle gap)
58	- Main Figures: F1 (Method Pipeline), F2 (SC Accuracy vs SICA Δ scatter: hero figure showing regime boundary, x=SC%, y=Δpp, with significance markers — the core visual for regime-dependent trap), F3 (Per-problem constraint precision: high-SC model vs low-SC model comparison, showing confirmation bias mechanism), F4 (C0 × SC two-dimensional diagnostic heatmap)
59	- Appendix: K ablation (exp-002), Temperature ablation (C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime), Per-domain detailed results, Overhead analysis (exp-009), Case studies (exp-008), QwQ-32B/DeepSeek-R1 negative control data, 6 remediation strategy details
60	
61	## Claim Risk Notes (Updated D052 — regime-dependent framing)
62	- **McNemar RESOLVED**: p=0.013 confirmed (b=12, c=2, programmatic computation). Three manual attempts all had transcription errors.
63	- **Claim 1 regime boundary — RESOLVED (D055)**: Sharp boundary at ~55% SC. Within-model ICL transition (D053) is decisive: same Mistral-7B, SC 54%→61.5%, SICA flips from +4.90pp→-2.23pp. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%, Δ=-2.45pp) is ABOVE the boundary, consistent with sharp threshold. All SC>60% conditions show Δ∈[-2.45, +0.49] n.s. The boundary is sharp, not gradual.
64	- **Claim 2 confirmation bias mechanism**: Hypothesis needs per-problem precision analysis comparing Mistral-7B vs Qwen-14B. OPEN. Also: Mistral contradiction rate 3.5% (128/204 problems with contradictions) vs Qwen ~1.5% — higher contradiction rate = more MaxSAT operating room in low-SC regime. **Variable naming inconsistency** (exp-036b): 56.4% of cross-trace answer pairs have zero shared Z3 variables, preventing meaningful cross-trace comparison. This is a key mechanism of the capability trap — even when constraints exist, inconsistent variable naming makes them incomparable across traces.
65	- **Claim 3 two-dimensional diagnostic**: C0 × SC theoretically motivated but empirically thin. Temperature ablation confirms C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime. Need low-SC C0 variation data. OPEN.
66	- **Claim 4 oracle gap**: Mistral +4.90pp vs Oracle +14.22pp = ~35% recovery. Compelling evidence extraction quality is bottleneck even when trap partially lifted.
67	- **exp-011 +10pp is noise**: n=30 SE≈9pp. exp-020 same task n=204 shows -2.4pp. Do NOT cite.
68	- **F2 hero figure redesign**: SC accuracy (x) vs SICA Δ (y) scatter with all model-domain conditions. Need exp-038 LogiQA + exp-035 Qwen-7B SC for more data points. OPEN.
69	- **RESOLVED (D055) — regime boundary is accuracy-dependent, not model-specific**: Within-model ICL transition (D053) controls for model architecture: same Mistral-7B, only SC changes (54%→61.5%), SICA flips sign. LLaMA-8B SC=63.2% is above boundary, consistent. Framing: sharp threshold at ~55% SC, below which constraints retain partial independence. No fallback needed.
70	
71	## Required Citations (Dream D037 — minimum 30 references)
72	
73	Paper Agent MUST include these categories (non-exhaustive list of must-cite works):
74	- **SC & extensions**: Wang et al. 2023 (SC), Chen et al. 2023 (USC), Loo et al. 2025 (SC scaling plateaus), Taubenfeld et al. 2025 ACL Findings (confidence-weighted SC), DiVeRSe (Li et al. 2023)
75	- **Neuro-symbolic reasoning (positive results on same benchmarks)**: DyLoS (EACL 2026), Aristotle (ACL 2025), FoVer (TACL 2025), LogicAgent (2025), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023)
76	- **Test-time compute scaling**: Snell et al. 2025 (ICLR Oral), DeepSeek-R1 technical report (Nature 2025), Diversity-Aware Training for TTS (2025)
77	- **Verification & reranking**: ORM (Cobbe et al. 2021), PRM (Lightman et al. 2023), Math-Shepherd (Wang et al. 2024)
78	- **Constraint satisfaction in NLP**: SATBench (EMNLP 2025), LLM Meets Constraint Propagation (IJCAI 2025)
79	- **Negative results in NLP**: appropriate precedents for analysis/negative-result papers
80	- **Debate & multi-agent**: Liang et al. 2023, Du et al. 2023
81	- **Reasoning model paradigm**: QwQ technical blog, o1 (OpenAI 2024)
82	
83	## Claim Scoping Rules (Dream D037)
84	
85	- n=60 conditions: present as "suggestive/consistent with" NOT "confirming." Only n≥200 conditions can "confirm" null.
86	- Combined N>1,300: acceptable if clearly stated that 804/1300 come from powered Qwen-14B condition.
87	- Domain coverage: acknowledge that SICA operates meaningfully (C0>20%) primarily on formal logic; other domains are degenerate (C0<10%) due to model capability.
88	- "Capability trap" framing: position as systematic quantification of a known challenge (not discovery). Our specific contributions: (a) per-problem precision analysis at n=307 granularity, (b) constraint ablation proving MAX-SAT ≈ counting, (c) C0 as deployment diagnostic.
89	
90	## Venue Fit Risks
91	- EMNLP: Analysis paper with both positive and negative results. Stronger venue fit than pure negative: characterizes WHEN symbolic aggregation helps (practitioner value). "So what should practitioners do?" → C0 + SC accuracy two-dimensional diagnostic + overhead analysis.
92	- Cross-discipline: MAX-SAT needs clear explanation for NLP audience.
93	- Statistical power: Strong (N>1,200 combined; Mistral positive n=204 with p=0.013).
94	- Novelty upgrade: Regime-dependent finding is more publishable than "it never works" — provides actionable boundary condition.
```

```json
      "content": "# Paper Framing — symb_invariant_consensus\n\n## Target Venue\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25)\n- Deadline: May 25, 2026 (ARR submission)\n\n## Core Claims (4 条) — D052 Regime-Dependent Capability Trap (REVISED)\n\n1. **The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap**: LLM self-extracted FOL constraint aggregation faces a regime-dependent tension. When model accuracy is high (SC ≥ ~70%), self-extracted constraints correlate strongly with the model's own answers (confirmation bias), losing discriminative power and reducing MAX-SAT to counting. When model accuracy is low (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, enabling SICA to provide genuine gains. The trap is not universal but accuracy-conditioned. Validated across 3 reasoning domains × 5 model families spanning instruction-scale, distilled-reasoning, and full-reasoning architectures. [Scope: this finding characterizes same-model self-extraction pipelines; methods using external verifiers or trained extractors may not share this limitation.]\n2. **Regime Boundary — SC Accuracy Determines SICA Effectiveness**: (a) **High-accuracy regime (trap active)**: Qwen-14B SC=75.49% Δ=+0.25pp n.s. (n=804), Qwen-14B LogiQA SC=78.8% Δ=0.0pp (n=151 interim), LLaMA-8B StrategyQA Δ=+2.0pp n.s. (n=200), LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204), DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B Δ=-3.3pp (n=60). Full reasoning model QwQ-32B C0<5% (degenerate). (b) **Low-accuracy regime (trap partially lifted)**: Mistral-7B SC=54.41% Δ=**+4.90pp p=0.013** (n=204, b=12 c=2; McNemar confirmed by programmatic computation, three manual attempts all had transcription errors). BUT: LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204) — need LLaMA-8B SC to determine if this is also low-SC (which would complicate the simple accuracy-threshold story) or medium-SC. The boundary may involve more than just SC accuracy (model architecture, constraint extraction quality, variable naming consistency).\n3. **C0 as Necessary-but-Insufficient Diagnostic**: C0 reliably predicts when symbolic consensus CANNOT help (C0<5% → method degenerates to SC), but high C0 does NOT guarantee gains. C0 combined with SC accuracy forms a two-dimensional screening: low C0 → degenerate; high C0 + high SC → trap; high C0 + low SC → potential gains. Zero-cost early stopping criterion for practitioners.\n4. **Oracle Proves Aggregation Mechanism, Localizes Bottleneck**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT achieves +14.22pp over SC (89.71% vs 75.49%, p=2.0e-7), proving the aggregation paradigm IS effective with discriminative constraints. Even Mistral-7B's significant +4.90pp captures only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality remains the binding bottleneck across all regimes. Six remediation strategies all failed to close this gap (contrastive prompting, cross-model extraction, generational scaling, solver-guided refinement, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT).\n\n## Core Insight\nLLM self-extracted FOL constraints exhibit a **regime-dependent capability trap**: their discriminative power depends on the extracting model's own accuracy on the task. When a model is strong (SC ≥ ~70%), its self-extracted constraints are strongly correlated with its own answers (confirmation bias), losing the independence needed to adjudicate between candidates. MAX-SAT over such constraints degenerates to counting. When a model is weak (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, and SICA can provide significant gains (Mistral-7B: +4.90pp, p=0.013, b=12 c=2). However, even in the favorable regime, self-extracted constraints capture only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality as the persistent bottleneck.\n\n**Key evidence**:\n- **Oracle control (exp-031)**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown-type questions +31.9pp. Proves aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints.\n- **Regime boundary**: Qwen-14B (SC=75.49%) Δ=+0.25pp n.s. vs Mistral-7B (SC=54.41%) Δ=+4.90pp p=0.013. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%) Δ=-2.45pp shows the boundary is sharp (not all weak models benefit).\n- **Within-model regime transition (D053)**: ICL Oracle on Mistral-7B (exp-044 partial n=148) boosted SC from 54.4%→61.5% via oracle examples, but SICA flipped from +4.90pp to -2.23pp. Same model, same architecture, only SC accuracy changed. This controls for model-specific factors and directly demonstrates the regime transition mechanism.\n- **Six remediation failures**: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model extraction (exp-030, 0.00pp), generational scaling (exp-027, +0.49pp n.s.), solver-guided refinement (exp-036, 0.9% UNSAT trigger rate), consistency filtering (constraints too sparse), cross-trace UNSAT (56.4% zero shared variables). All failed to close the extraction-oracle gap, confirming the trap is structural across prompting, architecture, capability, and verification dimensions.\n\nWe provide the first systematic empirical study (a) identifying the regime boundary where self-extracted constraint aggregation transitions from ineffective to beneficial, (b) localizing the failure mode to extraction quality via oracle control, (c) quantifying constraint non-discrimination at per-problem granularity, and (d) proposing C0 + SC accuracy as a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic. [Positioning: Logic-LM and LINC documented that LLM-to-FOL translation is error-prone; our contribution is the oracle-controlled causal separation of extraction vs aggregation failure, the regime boundary characterization, and systematic quantification across 5 model families.]\n\n## Story Spine\n- **Problem**: Recent work proposes augmenting LLM reasoning with symbolic methods, extracting formal constraints from reasoning traces and using solvers for answer selection. But when should practitioners invest in such methods vs. simple majority voting?\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: SC (Wang et al. 2023), USC (Chen et al. 2023), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023), ORM/PRM (Cobbe et al. 2021, Lightman et al. 2023), Weighted SC (Taubenfeld et al. 2024). None systematically characterize when symbolic aggregation helps vs. when it degenerates to voting.\n- **Our framing**: We instantiate symbolic consensus as SICA (MAX-SAT over extracted FOL constraints) and systematically evaluate across 3 domains × 5 model families + 1 full reasoning model. We discover a regime-dependent capability trap: SICA gains are accuracy-conditioned, not universally absent.\n- **Headline result**: \"Across 5 model families, SICA's effectiveness depends on the extracting model's baseline accuracy. Strong models (SC ≥ 70%) show no gain (Δ = -3.3pp to +2.0pp, all n.s.); a weaker model (Mistral-7B, SC = 54%) shows significant improvement (+4.90pp, p = 0.013). Oracle control (+14.22pp) proves the aggregation mechanism works with discriminative constraints, but even the best self-extraction result captures only ~35% of this ceiling. Six remediation strategies all fail to close the gap. We identify a confirmation-bias mechanism: stronger models' constraints mirror their own answers, losing discriminative power. C0 + SC accuracy form a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic.\"\n\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Defensive Experiments\n1. **\"Gains not statistically significant\"** → Four powered tests: exp-001 (n=804), exp-019 (n=200), exp-020 (n=204), exp-010 (n=60). ```text
```
```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Scaling test-time computation has emerged as a key strategy for improving LLM reasoning \citep{snell2025scaling}. As the dominant aggregation method, majority voting, faces diminishing returns from additional traces, verification-based approaches that extract richer signal offer a path to break this plateau. Symbolic verification through formal constraints and satisfiability solvers is one promising direction, as it provides logical guarantees that natural language self-critique lacks.
4	
5	Neuro-symbolic methods that augment large language model (LLM) reasoning with formal solvers have demonstrated gains on logical reasoning benchmarks. Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} delegate formalization to external provers. More recent systems route problems to specialized solver paradigms \citep{xu2026dylos}, apply logic-complete decomposition \citep{li2025aristotle}, train dedicated first-order logic (FOL) verifiers on supervised data \citep{wang2025fover}, or employ multi-perspective grounding \citep{luo2025logicagent}. These systems report positive results on formal reasoning benchmarks, but a critical commonality unites them: each introduces capabilities beyond the reasoning model itself, whether trained verifiers, multi-paradigm solver selection, or external knowledge bases. The external machinery is effective but imposes practical costs in training data, integration complexity, and domain-specific engineering that limits portability.
6	
7	These results raise a direct question: can the reasoning model itself extract useful formal constraints from its own traces? Same-model self-extraction would require no training data, no external prover, and no second model. There is reason to expect this approach could succeed where natural language self-critique fails \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}: FOL eliminates the ambiguity of natural language verification, and the downstream solver (Z3) provides formally guaranteed soundness, so any correctly extracted constraint contributes provably valid reasoning. Practitioners could augment any LLM with symbolic verification by adding a single extraction prompt and a satisfiability solver, with no domain-specific infrastructure. If successful, this approach would provide a lightweight, portable alternative to the external machinery that current systems require. Yet no prior work has systematically evaluated whether self-extraction produces constraints useful for answer aggregation, or characterized the conditions under which it succeeds or fails. This gap is consequential: the practical promise of neuro-symbolic reasoning hinges on whether the reasoning model alone can supply formal structure of sufficient quality, or whether external grounding is fundamentally required.
8	
9	Self-Consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across multiple chain-of-thought samples \citep{wang2023sc} and remains competitive across mathematical, logical, and commonsense reasoning \citep{chen2024usc, taubenfeld2025cisc}. SC treats traces as black boxes, discarding all intermediate reasoning. Each trace, however, contains intermediate steps, logical dependencies, and structural relationships that could in principle ground aggregation in formal logic, adding discriminative power beyond vote counting. This would also address SC's known scaling plateau, where additional traces yield diminishing returns \citep{loo2025reevaluating}.
10	
11	We evaluate this possibility through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), a pipeline that prompts a single LLM to extract FOL-style constraints (in practice predominantly ground propositional clauses; \S\ref{sec:constraint-extraction}) from $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicates by syntactic equivalence, and solves a weighted partial MAX-SAT problem via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3}. The answer best supported by the largest satisfiable constraint subset is selected (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). We test six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters (Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, QwQ-32B), including instruction-tuned models, a reasoning-specialized model, and a distilled reasoning model, across three reasoning domains: formal logic (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}), commonsense (StrategyQA \citep{geva2021strategyqa}), and science (ARC-Challenge \citep{clark2018arc}), with supplementary probes on GSM8K \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} and AIME 2024, totaling more than 1,500 instances. Oracle controls on FOLIO ($n=204$) and ProofWriter ($n=600$) replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations verified by Z3, isolating extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness following established methodology \citep{press2023compositionality}.
12	
13	Across all conditions, SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over SC, with accuracy gaps between $-3.3$ and $+3.3$\,pp. The oracle experiments yield a fundamentally different result: $+8.82$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound when given discriminative constraints; the bottleneck is extraction quality. Because the same model generates both answers and constraints, the extracted constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and carry no independent signal. We term this the \emph{capability trap}: the model cannot formalize corrections to its own reasoning errors because extraction inherits the same biases that produced those errors. A constraint ablation confirms the diagnosis: MAX-SAT and count-only aggregation agree on 803 of 804 predictions, and SICA's 31 disagreements with SC approximately cancel (16 wins, 14 losses, 1 both-wrong with different answers), yielding a net gain of only $+$2 problems (\S\ref{sec:ablation}). Full-dataset analysis shows only 8.2\% of unique constraints are semantically discriminative across candidates (95\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$); the remainder encode shared domain facts that contribute identical scores to every answer. Constraint extraction accounts for 48.7\% of total SICA pipeline time, roughly doubling wall-clock cost for zero expected accuracy gain.
14	
15	Our contributions are:
16	\begin{itemize}
17	\item We characterize the capability trap in same-model constraint extraction: across six models spanning three base architectures and three reasoning domains ($N > 1{,}500$), self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over majority voting. Dual-metric analysis reveals that 39.2\% of constraint weight is statistically correlated with source answers, yet only 8.2\% of constraints are semantically discriminative under Z3 (95\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$). This structural disconnect between statistical association and semantic content has not been identified in prior self-verification analyses (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}, \S\ref{sec:ablation}).
18	\item We introduce a component-isolation evaluation framework and apply it via oracle controls on two datasets: replacing self-extraction with gold annotations yields $+8.82$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism is sound and the bottleneck lies primarily in extraction quality. This oracle-replacement methodology generalizes to any neuro-symbolic pipeline where an LLM extracts formal structure from its own outputs (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
19	\item We quantify the recoverable gap: the $+8.82$/$+11.50$\,pp oracle ceilings and the 48.7\% cost overhead of non-discriminative extraction jointly establish the barrier and the potential for future methods targeting the extraction bottleneck (\S\ref{sec:ablation}, \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
20	\item We propose the multi-candidate rate $C_0$ as a zero-cost, necessary-condition diagnostic for practitioners evaluating symbolic aggregation (\S\ref{sec:c0}).
21	\end{itemize}
22	
```

_source line 2391 · 2026-05-18T20:24:09.179Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NG3fzcCS3vsNerV6LH12gy`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Neuro-symbolic methods propose extracting first-order logic (FOL) constraints from Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning traces to improve answer aggregation beyond majority voting. Whether the reasoning model itself can produce useful constraints through self-extraction has not been systematically evaluated. If self-extraction works, it eliminates the need for trained verifiers, external provers, or domain-specific infrastructure. We evaluate Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which formulates a weighted partial Maximum Satisfiability (MAX-SAT) problem over self-extracted FOL constraints, across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters, including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains with more than 1,500 total instances. We include oracle controls on two datasets that replace self-extraction with gold annotations verified by Z3 to isolate extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness. Self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over Self-Consistency (SC) in any tested condition, with accuracy gaps ranging from $-3.3$ to $+3.3$ percentage points. The oracle variants achieve $+8.82$ and $+11.50$ percentage points over SC on FOLIO and ProofWriter respectively (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism is sound when supplied with high-quality constraints. We characterize this failure as a \emph{capability trap}: because the same model that produced potentially flawed reasoning also extracts the constraints, those constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and carry no independent discriminative signal. Only 8.2\% of self-extracted constraints are semantically discriminative across candidate answers (95\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$). Within the 7B--32B range tested, same-model constraint extraction does not improve LLM answer aggregation without independent sources of formal knowledge.
3	\end{abstract}
```

</details>

```json
    "file": {
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nNeuro-symbolic methods propose extracting first-order logic (FOL) constraints from Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning traces to improve answer aggregation beyond majority voting. Whether the reasoning model itself can produce useful constraints through self-extraction has not been systematically evaluated. If self-extraction works, it eliminates the need for trained verifiers, external provers, or domain-specific infrastructure. We evaluate Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which formulates a weighted partial Maximum Satisfiability (MAX-SAT) problem over self-extracted FOL constraints, across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters, including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains with more than 1,500 total instances. We include oracle controls on two datasets that replace self-extraction with gold annotations verified by Z3 to isolate extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness. Self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over Self-Consistency (SC) in any tested condition, with accuracy gaps ranging from $-3.3$ to $+3.3$ percentage points. The oracle variants achieve $+8.82$ and $+11.50$ percentage points over SC on FOLIO and ProofWriter respectively (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism is sound when supplied with high-quality constraints. We characterize this failure as a \\emph{capability trap}: because the same model that produced potentially flawed reasoning also extracts the constraints, those constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and carry no independent discriminative signal. Only 8.2\\% of self-extracted constraints are semantically discriminative across candidate answers (95\\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$). Within the 7B--32B range tested, same-model constraint extraction does not improve LLM answer aggregation without independent sources of formal knowledge.\n\\end{abstract}",
      "numLines": 3,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 3
    }
  },
```

```text
- exp-033 (Mistral-7B FOLIO-204): SICA 59.31%, SC 54.41%, Δ=+4.90pp, p=0.013 ✓

- exp-044 (ICL Oracle) failed and the data isn't usable.

I'm noticing something odd with exp-041 though — it shows a significant negative effect, but the Director's table has different numbers for Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO-204. Looks like exp-041 was a rerun on a different server, so it's a separate experiment from the original.

The Director's table appears to be my authoritative source here, so I should stick with those numbers rather than getting caught up in the discrepancies across individual experiment runs. For the Mistral-7B+ICL result the Director mentioned, that's likely partial data from exp-044 since the paper notes it only had n=148 samples.

Rather than spending more time validating every data point, I should move forward with the rewrite plan using the Director's consolidated table. The specific data questions can get sorted out during execution.

Now I'm laying out the key structural decisions: I'll reorganize Table 1 by performance regime instead of grouping by model, use the oracle improvement of +14.22pp that the Director confirmed, pull the SC baselines directly from their table, and incorporate the new experimental results like the Mistral FOLIO-204 and LogiQA findings.

For the remaining gaps—the full PW-600 results and the constraint precision comparison—I'll mark those as pending and fill them in once available. I'm also planning to expand Section 4.6 on targeted interventions by adding three more remediation strategies to reach six total.

Let me finalize this plan and get it to the Director for approval.
```

```text
Now I have key additional data:

- **exp-035**: This is Qwen2.5-7B FOLIO-204: SC=77.34%, SICA=76.85%, Δ=-0.49pp, p=1.0, n=203 (not 204 - 1 excluded). So the Director's "Qwen2.5-7B FOLIO-204 SC=77.34%" comes from exp-035.

Wait, exp-035 was labeled as ARC-Challenge in my earlier registry read. Let me check again - the status showed "RUNNING" earlier but now "DONE". 

The metrics clearly show n=203 with SC=77.34%, which matches Qwen2.5-7B on FOLIO-204, not ARC-Challenge where the score would be much higher. There's a discrepancy between the hypothesis I saw earlier mentioning ARC-Challenge and what the actual metrics reveal about the dataset being tested.

Looking at the Director's data table as the source of truth, I'm seeing the remediation strategies didn't yield meaningful improvements: Z3 feedback had negligible impact with only 0.9% UNSAT rate, cross-trace UNSAT showed 56.4% zero shared variables with no accuracy gain, and the consistency filtering thresholds all converged to Self-Consistency or worse. The six remediation approaches from the paper framing—starting with contrastive prompting showing a -1.1pp drop and cross-model extraction—appear to be underperforming.

Given the 7-day deadline, I need to prioritize strategically: focus on experiments first, then reshape the discussion and introduction around these findings, followed by the abstract, conclusion, and related work. The key structural change involves rebuilding Table 1 with new rows including Mistral-7B FOLIO-204 results.

Updating the table with the latest experimental data...

I need to consolidate the results, removing the smaller partial runs and organizing everything by model accuracy to better illustrate where the regime transition occurs. There's also a new section emerging around within-model in-context learning transitions that should use SC accuracy as the explanatory mechanism. The remediation strategies table needs to expand from three to six approaches, and I'm revising the oracle performance numbers upward based on fresh calculations. The discussion will need to address how the regime boundary actually works mechanistically, treating it as a two-dimensional diagnostic problem.

The scatter plot showing SC accuracy versus performance delta is going to be the centerpiece figure here. But I'm noticing a discrepancy in the Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO-204 numbers—the Director's table shows SC at 75.49% with a delta of +0.25pp, but my three separate experiments give me different values that don't quite align with that aggregate figure, including one with a significant p-value of 0.0455.

What's particularly striking is that exp-041 shows SICA actually performing worse than SC on this configuration, which is a negative result that fits the pattern I'm seeing—when the model is already high-accuracy and the trap is active, SICA becomes a liability rather than a help. Rather than get caught up validating every data point, I should flag these inconsistencies in my report to the Director and move forward with the plan.
```

[Director 指令] [Patch — 重要新证据] Entropy-conditioned constraint precision 分析完成，是论文核心证据之一。请在 Reframe 中整合。

## 新增证据：Entropy-Conditioned Analysis

Per-problem answer entropy 分析揭示了 regime boundary 的 MECHANISM：

| Entropy 区间 | Mistral-7B (n, Δ) | Qwen-14B (n, Δ) |
|-------------|-------------------|-----------------|
| Low (0-0.5) | n=65, 0.0pp | n=135, 0.0pp |
| Medium (0.5-1.0) | n=66, +4.5pp | n=48, -4.2pp |
| High (1.0+) | n=73, **+9.6pp** | n=21, **+4.8pp** |

**关键洞察**：Conditioned on high entropy，两个模型的 SICA 都有正收益。差异在于高 entropy 问题的比例：Mistral 35.8% vs Qwen 10.3%。

**Capability trap 量化**：
- Unanimous problems: 18.6% (Mistral) vs 58.8% (Qwen)
- Avg entropy: 0.815 vs 0.350
- Override precision: 70.6% vs 28.6%
- Confirmation bias (>80%): 31.9% vs 62.3%
- MaxSAT sat ratio: 0.961 vs 0.991 (solver triviality)

**因果链**：强模型 → traces 一致 → constraints 冗余 → solver trivial → SICA≡SC

这个分析应成为 T5 (Entropy-Conditioned Analysis) 和 F3 (Entropy vs SICA Δ bar chart) 的数据来源。paper_framing.md 已更新。

[Director 指令] [Data Confirmation] 回答你的 3 个问题 + 执行计划审批

### Q1: Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO-204 用哪个 run？

**主表用 exp-001 (sica_expanded_14b)**：n=804 combined (PW+FOLIO), SC=76.2%, SICA=76.5%, Δ=+0.3pp。这是 powered test (n=804)。

**Per-dataset breakdown** 报告 FOLIO-204 两个独立 run：
- exp-026 (canonical, determinism-fixed): SC=75.00%, SICA=74.51%, Δ=-0.49pp
- exp-041 (replication, westd-16639): SC=77.45%, SICA=75.49%, Δ=-1.96pp, exact p=0.125 (NOT 0.045)

注意：Director 表格中 "SC=75.49% Δ=+0.25pp" 来自 exp-001 combined 804 题，不是单独 FOLIO-204。

### Q2: exp-041 显著负结果 (p=0.045)?

**不显著**。Agent 的 p=0.045 是错的（χ² 无连续性校正，n=4 discordant 不适用）。精确 McNemar：b=0, c=4, exact p=0.125。方向一致（SICA < SC），但不显著。

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is a Director orchestrating a Paper Agent for the `symb_invariant_consensus` ML research project targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25 (~7 day deadline). The session covers:
   - Completing a 折中方案 (compromise approach): adding TTS paragraph to Discussion, verifying references
   - Adding §4.6 Targeted Interventions with Qwen3 generational data + three negative results
   - Executing cascade updates to conclusion.tex and discussion.tex (cross-model negative result)
   - **MAJOR REFRAME (D052/D055)**: Shifting entire paper framing from "universal capability trap (SICA never works)" to "regime-dependent capability trap (SICA's effectiveness depends on model SC accuracy)"
   - Integrating entropy-conditioned analysis as mechanism evidence
   - Planning and beginning execution of full paper rewrite for all sections except Method (§3)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **SICA pipeline**: K traces → FOL constraint extraction → deduplication → weighted partial MAX-SAT (Z3) → answer selection
   - **Self-Consistency (SC)**: majority voting over K traces
   - **Regime-dependent capability trap** (NEW FRAMING): SICA effectiveness depends on model's SC accuracy
     - Low SC (≤~55%): constraints retain partial independence → SICA helps (Mistral +4.90pp, p=0.013)
     - High SC (≥~60%): confirmation bias → constraints redundant → SICA ≈ SC
     - Sharp boundary at ~55-60% SC
   - **Oracle-SICA**: gold FOL annotations + Z3 → +14.22pp FOLIO (exp-031, p=2.0e-7), +11.50pp PW (exp-022)
   - **Entropy-conditioned analysis**: per-problem answer entropy reveals mechanism — high entropy problems benefit from SICA for BOTH models; distribution difference explains aggregate regime effect
   - **Six remediation strategies**: all failed (contrastive, cross-model, generational, Z3 feedback, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT)
   - **C0 + SC two-dimensional diagnostic**: C0 alone is necessary-but-insufficient; combined with SC accuracy forms deployment screening
   - **recovery_rate** = Δ_method / Δ_oracle × 100% (Mistral recovers ~35% of oracle ceiling)
   - McNemar exact test for significance, bootstrap CI for n≥200

3. Files and Code Sections:
   
   - `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex`
     - Core discussion section, modified twice in this session
     - **Edit 1**: Added `\paragraph{Implications for Test-Time Scaling.}` at end:
       ```latex
       \paragraph{Implications for Test-Time Scaling.}
       Scaling test-time computation through additional reasoning traces faces diminishing returns under majority voting \citep{snell2025scaling, loo2025reevaluating}. Our oracle results demonstrate that symbolic verification can break this plateau: gold FOL constraints yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, extracting substantial signal from the same traces that majority voting cannot use. The bottleneck is not the verification mechanism but the constraint source. Self-extracted constraints inherit the correlated errors of the traces they were derived from, producing what \citet{kambhampati2024position} term ``fake verification'' where the verifier cannot catch errors the generator makes. Z3 Feedback Refinement (\S\ref{sec:z3-feedback}) represents one attempt to break this circularity through the LLM-Modulo paradigm, where an external solver provides corrective signals that the model cannot generate through introspection. Whether this partial external grounding suffices to improve constraint discriminativeness remains an open empirical question (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}). For practitioners, $C_0$ offers a zero-cost signal for whether test-time scaling has reached the majority-voting ceiling: when $C_0 < 5\%$, additional traces provide no new information, and investing in verification infrastructure rather than more samples becomes the rational allocation of test-time compute.
       ```
     - **Edit 2**: Cascade update #2 — changed cross-model from future path to verified negative:

The remaining paths target the extraction bottleneck directly;"

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "0aaad82b-c0d9-46ed-b8f3-62ca36bbfbce",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nScaling test-time computation has emerged as a key strategy for improving LLM reasoning \\citep{snell2025scaling}. As the dominant aggregation method, majority voting, faces diminishing returns from additional traces, verification-based approaches that extract richer signal offer a path to break this plateau. Symbolic verification through formal constraints and satisfiability solvers is one promising direction, as it provides logical guarantees that natural language self-critique lacks.\n\nNeuro-symbolic methods that augment large language model (LLM) reasoning with formal solvers have demonstrated gains on logical reasoning benchmarks. Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} delegate formalization to external provers. More recent systems route problems to specialized solver paradigms \\citep{xu2026dylos}, apply logic-complete decomposition \\citep{li2025aristotle}, train dedicated first-order logic (FOL) verifiers on supervised data \\citep{wang2025fover}, or employ multi-perspective grounding \\citep{luo2025logicagent}. These systems report positive results on formal reasoning benchmarks, but a critical commonality unites them: each introduces capabilities beyond the reasoning model itself, whether trained verifiers, multi-paradigm solver selection, or external knowledge bases. The external machinery is effective but imposes practical costs in training data, integration complexity, and domain-specific engineering that limits portability.\n\nThese results raise a direct question: can the reasoning model itself extract useful formal constraints from its own traces? Same-model self-extraction would require no training data, no external prover, and no second model. There is reason to expect this approach could succeed where natural language self-critique fails \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}: FOL eliminates the ambiguity of natural language verification, and the downstream solver (Z3) provides formally guaranteed soundness, so any correctly extracted constraint contributes provably valid reasoning. Practitioners could augment any LLM with symbolic verification by adding a single extraction prompt and a satisfiability solver, with no domain-specific infrastructure. If successful, this approach would provide a lightweight, portable alternative to the external machinery that current systems require. Yet no prior work has systematically evaluated whether self-extraction produces constraints useful for answer aggregation, or characterized the conditions under which it succeeds or fails. This gap is consequential: the practical promise of neuro-symbolic reasoning hinges on whether the reasoning model alone can supply formal structure of sufficient quality, or whether external grounding is fundamentally required.\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across multiple chain-of-thought samples \\citep{wang2023sc} and remains competitive across mathematical, logical, and commonsense reasoning \\citep{chen2024usc, taubenfeld2025cisc}. SC treats traces as black boxes, discarding all intermediate reasoning. Each trace, however, contains intermediate steps, logical dependencies, and structural relationships that could in principle ground aggregation in formal logic, adding discriminative power beyond vote counting. This would also address SC's known scaling plateau, where additional traces yield diminishing returns \\citep{loo2025reevaluating}.\n\nWe evaluate this possibility through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), a pipeline that prompts a single LLM to extract FOL-style constraints (in practice predominantly ground propositional clauses; \\S\\ref{sec:constraint-extraction}) from $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicates by syntactic equivalence, and solves a weighted partial MAX-SAT problem via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3}. The answer best supported by the largest satisfiable constraint subset is selected (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). We test six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters (Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, QwQ-32B), including instruction-tuned models, a reasoning-specialized model, and a distilled reasoning model, across three reasoning domains: formal logic (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}), commonsense (StrategyQA \\citep{geva2021strategyqa}), and science (ARC-Challenge \\citep{clark2018arc}), with supplementary probes on GSM8K \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} and AIME 2024, totaling more than 1,500 instances. Oracle controls on FOLIO ($n=204$) and ProofWriter ($n=600$) replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations verified by Z3, isolating extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness following established methodology \\citep{press2023compositionality}.\n\nAcross all conditions, SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over SC, with accuracy gaps between $-3.3$ and $+3.3$\\,pp. The oracle experiments yield a fundamentally different result: $+8.82$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound when given discriminative constraints; the bottleneck is extraction quality. Because the same model generates both answers and constraints, the extracted constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and carry no independent signal. We term this the \\emph{capability trap}: the model cannot formalize corrections to its own reasoning errors because extraction inherits the same biases that produced those errors. A constraint ablation confirms the diagnosis: MAX-SAT and count-only aggregation agree on 803 of 804 predictions, and SICA's 31 disagreements with SC approximately cancel (16 wins, 14 losses, 1 both-wrong with different answers), yielding a net gain of only $+$2 problems (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}). Full-dataset analysis shows only 8.2\\% of unique constraints are semantically discriminative across candidates (95\\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$); the remainder encode shared domain facts that contribute identical scores to every answer. Constraint extraction accounts for 48.7\\% of total SICA pipeline time, roughly doubling wall-clock cost for zero expected accuracy gain.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item We characterize the capability trap in same-model constraint extraction: across six models spanning three base architectures and three reasoning domains ($N > 1{,}500$), self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over majority voting. Dual-metric analysis reveals that 39.2\\% of constraint weight is statistically correlated with source answers, yet only 8.2\\% of constraints are semantically discriminative under Z3 (95\\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$). This structural disconnect between statistical association and semantic content has not been identified in prior self-verification analyses (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}, \\S\\ref{sec:ablation}).\n\\item We introduce a component-isolation evaluation framework and apply it via oracle controls on two datasets: replacing self-extraction with gold annotations yields $+8.82$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism is sound and the bottleneck lies primarily in extraction quality. This oracle-replacement methodology generalizes to any neuro-symbolic pipeline where an LLM extracts formal structure from its own outputs (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\\item We quantify the recoverable gap: the $+8.82$/$+11.50$\\,pp oracle ceilings and the 48.7\\% cost overhead of non-discriminative extraction jointly establish the barrier and the potential for future methods targeting the extraction bottleneck (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\\item We propose the multi-candidate rate $C_0$ as a zero-cost, necessary-condition diagnostic for practitioners evaluating symbolic aggregation (\\S\\ref{sec:c0}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
        "numLines": 22,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 22
      }
    },
```

```json
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nNeuro-symbolic methods propose extracting first-order logic (FOL) constraints from Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning traces to improve answer aggregation beyond majority voting. Whether the reasoning model itself can produce useful constraints through self-extraction has not been systematically evaluated. If self-extraction works, it eliminates the need for trained verifiers, external provers, or domain-specific infrastructure. We evaluate Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which formulates a weighted partial Maximum Satisfiability (MAX-SAT) problem over self-extracted FOL constraints, across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters, including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains with more than 1,500 total instances. We include oracle controls on two datasets that replace self-extraction with gold annotations verified by Z3 to isolate extraction quality from aggregation effectiveness. Self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over Self-Consistency (SC) in any tested condition, with accuracy gaps ranging from $-3.3$ to $+3.3$ percentage points. The oracle variants achieve $+8.82$ and $+11.50$ percentage points over SC on FOLIO and ProofWriter respectively (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism is sound when supplied with high-quality constraints. We characterize this failure as a \\emph{capability trap}: because the same model that produced potentially flawed reasoning also extracts the constraints, those constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and carry no independent discriminative signal. Only 8.2\\% of self-extracted constraints are semantically discriminative across candidate answers (95\\% CI [7.6, 8.8]; $N = 804$). Within the 7B--32B range tested, same-model constraint extraction does not improve LLM answer aggregation without independent sources of formal knowledge.\n\\end{abstract}",
        "numLines": 3,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 3
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/abstract.tex"
  },
```

```json
{
```

```json
    "content": {
```
