      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe evaluated Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA) from 7B to 32B parameters, including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains with more than 1,500 total instances. Self-extracted SICA provides no statistically significant improvement over Self-Consistency in any tested condition, with accuracy gaps ranging from $-3.3$ to $+3.3$\\,pp. Oracle experiments replacing self-extraction with gold annotations yield $+8.82$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$). This result causally localizes the failure to extraction quality: the MAX-SAT aggregation mechanism is sound when supplied with high-quality constraints, but same-model self-extraction cannot produce them. We characterize this as a \\emph{capability trap}: because the reasoning model also extracts the constraints, those constraints inherit the same biases that produced potentially flawed answers and carry no independent discriminative signal. The failure is not one of aggregation design but of the circular dependency between generation and verification within a single model.\n\nThe practical consequences follow from the constraint quality analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}, \\S\\ref{sec:ablation}). Over 91\\% of self-extracted constraints are semantically non-discriminative (8.2\\% Z3 semantic discriminative rate, $N = 804$), which reduces MAX-SAT to a weighted vote count (803/804 agreement with count-only). The extraction step roughly doubles wall-clock cost for zero expected gain. For practitioners, the multi-candidate rate $C_0$ provides a zero-cost screening criterion computable from trace samples alone: low $C_0$ rules out any SICA benefit by construction, while high $C_0$ warrants a constraint quality audit before committing to full-scale extraction.\n\nThe oracle gaps ($+8.82$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, combined $N = 804$) quantify recoverable potential in formal logic domains; extension to commonsense and science domains requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. Trained extractors and external provers target the extraction bottleneck directly; cross-model extraction alone proves insufficient, as Mistral-7B extracting from Qwen2.5-14B traces yields identical performance to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:interventions}).",
        "numLines": 7,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 7
      }

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models spanning three base architectures (Qwen, Mistral, LLaMA), including instruction-tuned, reasoning-specialized, and distilled variants, and three reasoning domains, with two additional benchmarks as operating-regime probes.
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} We test Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct~\cite{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\cite{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\cite{dubey2024llama3}, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B~\cite{guo2025deepseekr1}, and QwQ-32B~\cite{qwen2024qwq}, spanning 7B to 32B parameters. The Qwen2.5-7B condition enables within-family scaling comparison.
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\cite{han2022folio} (validation split) provides formal logic problems with gold FOL annotations ($n=204$), enabling the oracle control (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). ProofWriter~\cite{tafjord2021proofwriter} tests multi-step deductive reasoning ($n=600$). StrategyQA~\cite{geva2021strategyqa} evaluates commonsense reasoning ($n=200$ for LLaMA-3.1-8B; $n=60$ for other models). ARC-Challenge~\cite{clark2018arc} covers science questions ($n=60$). GSM8K~\cite{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n=30$) and AIME 2024 ($n=22$) serve as operating-regime probes, both exhibiting near-ceiling SC accuracy or near-zero $C_0$.
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} We sample $K=12$ traces per question at temperature $T=0.7$. Constraint extraction uses a separate call at $T_{\text{ext}}=0.3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}). Z3~\cite{demoura2008z3} solves each MAX-SAT instance with a 10-second timeout. Temperature ablation varies $T \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Metrics.} We report accuracy (\%), $C_0$ (\S\ref{sec:c0}), and $\Delta = \text{SICA} - \text{SC}$ in percentage points (pp). Statistical significance uses the McNemar test at $\alpha = 0.05$. For conditions with $n \geq 200$, we compute 95\% bootstrap confidence intervals with 10{,}000 resamples.
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results: SICA Provides No Significant Gain}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports results across all model-domain conditions. Symbolic constraint aggregation does not improve over majority voting.
22	
23	\begin{table*}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Accuracy of Self-Consistency (SC) and SICA across three primary domains and two supplementary probes. $\Delta$ denotes the SICA$-$SC gap in percentage points. No self-extraction condition reaches statistical significance ($\alpha = 0.05$). The oracle upper bound replaces self-extracted constraints with gold FOL annotations verified by Z3. $\dagger$: $n \leq 60$, insufficient power for confirmatory conclusions (Qwen2.5-7B reports $n{=}56$ after 4 FOLIO problems were excluded due to pipeline processing failures). $\ddagger$: $n=30$ pilot, included for operating regime characterization. $^a$McNemar test for $n \geq 200$; exact binomial test on discordant pairs for $n \leq 60$.}
27	\label{tab:main_results}
28	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
29	\toprule
30	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p^a$ \\
31	\midrule
32	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 79.41 & 79.41 & 0.00 & --- \\
33	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & 75.50 & $+$0.33 & n.s. \\
34	Qwen2.5-14B & StrategyQA$^\dagger$ & 60 & 53.33 & 56.67 & $+$3.33 & 0.50 \\
35	Qwen2.5-14B & ARC-Chall.$^\dagger$ & 60 & 96.67 & 95.00 & $-$1.67 & --- \\
36	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 56 & 69.64 & 69.64 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
37	Mistral-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 60 & 48.33 & 48.33 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
38	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
39	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 63.24 & 60.78 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\
40	DeepSeek-R1-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 60 & 71.67 & 68.33 & $-$3.33 & 0.480 \\
41	QwQ-32B & AIME$^\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & --- \\
42	Qwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\ddagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & --- \\
43	\midrule
44	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold FOL/DSL + Z3 replaces self-extraction)}} \\
45	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 79.41 & \textbf{88.24} & $+$8.82 & $<$0.001 \\
46	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \textbf{86.67} & $+$11.50 & $<$0.001 \\
47	\bottomrule
48	\end{tabular}
49	\end{table*}
50	
51	The four confirmatory conditions ($n \geq 200$) span two models (Qwen2.5-14B and LLaMA-3.1-8B) across formal logic, commonsense, and deductive reasoning (combined $N = 1{,}208$). Across these conditions, $\Delta$ ranges from $-2.45$ to $+2.00$~pp, with no condition reaching significance ($p = 0.267$ and $0.424$ for the two nonzero gaps). The seven exploratory conditions ($n \leq 60$) cover the remaining four models (Qwen2.5-7B, Mistral-7B, DeepSeek-R1-7B, QwQ-32B) and are consistent with this null pattern: $\Delta$ ranges from $-3.33$ to $+3.33$~pp, all nonsignificant. QwQ-32B on AIME produces identical outputs ($C_0 = 2.6\%$): reasoning-specialized models leave no room for SICA to operate.
52	
53	Across eleven conditions, six models, and more than 1,500 instances (four confirmatory conditions with $n \geq 200$, seven exploratory conditions with $n \leq 60$), SICA does not improve upon majority voting. The oracle rows tell a different story: gold constraints yield $+8.82$~pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$~pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$), proving the aggregation mechanism works when given discriminative constraints.
54	
55	
56	\subsection{Oracle Control: The Aggregation Mechanism Is Effective}
57	\label{sec:oracle}
58	
59	We construct oracle variants that replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL formulas from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL logic programs from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). The oracle uses Z3 to evaluate each gold constraint against each candidate answer directly, rather than the source-trace attribution used in self-extraction mode (\S\ref{sec:maxsat}). This design isolates the constraint quality variable: any improvement is attributable to gold constraints, not scoring mechanics. The constraint ablation (\S\ref{sec:ablation}) independently confirms that the scoring mechanism is irrelevant under self-extraction (803/804 agreement with count-only).
60	
61	On FOLIO, Oracle-SICA achieves 88.24\% accuracy versus 79.41\% for both SC and standard SICA ($+8.82$~pp, McNemar $p = 6.15 \times 10^{-5}$). Z3 checks on gold annotations agree with ground-truth labels for 193/204 problems (94.6\%). On ProofWriter, Oracle-SICA achieves 86.67\% versus 75.17\% for SC ($+11.50$~pp, McNemar $p = 9.21 \times 10^{-15}$), with Z3 gold agreement of 97.1\% (577/594). The ProofWriter gain is larger and more significant, with relational subtypes benefiting most: RelNeg $+17.86$~pp, RelNoneg $+13.01$~pp (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:pw_oracle_subtype}). Across both datasets ($N = 804$), oracle constraints consistently and significantly improve aggregation. The bottleneck lies primarily in extraction quality.
62	
63	
64	\subsection{Capability Trap Diagnostics}
65	\label{sec:diagnostics}
66	
67	Why does SICA fail? Full per-condition metrics are in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:capability_trap}. $C_0$ ranges from 2.6\% (QwQ-32B) to 61.8\% (LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO). For reasoning-specialized models, over 97\% of questions receive unanimous answers, so SICA reduces to SC by construction. For instruction-tuned models, higher $C_0$ creates opportunities for SICA to differ from SC, yet $\Delta$ remains nonsignificant everywhere (Figure~\ref{fig:diagnostics}b). Extraction rates exceed 0.80 and contradiction rates stay below 7\%, so the pipeline is mechanically functional. The problem is extraction vacuity: constraints are syntactically valid but semantically redundant with their source answers. Even the highest-quality condition (LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO, extraction rate 0.94) yields $\Delta = -2.45$~pp. A temperature ablation ($T \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$, Qwen2.5-14B, $n{=}60$) shows $C_0$ increasing monotonically from 30.0\% ($T{=}0.3$) to 38.3\% ($T{=}0.5$), 40.0\% ($T{=}0.7$), and 43.3\% ($T{=}1.0$), yet $\Delta$ never exceeds zero: 0.0~pp at $T{=}0.3$, 0.0~pp at $T{=}0.5$, $-3.33$~pp at $T{=}0.7$ ($n{=}60$, n.s.), and 0.0~pp at $T{=}1.0$ (Figure~\ref{fig:diagnostics}a). Even at the default temperature $T{=}0.7$, SICA does not outperform SC.
68	
69	\begin{figure}[t]
70	\centering
71	\begin{subfigure}[b]{0.48\columnwidth}
72	\centering
73	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig2_capability_scatter.pdf}
74	\caption{Vote entropy vs.\ constraint exclusion rate (Qwen2.5-14B, $n{=}307$ multi-candidate problems).}
75	\label{fig:capability_scatter}
76	\end{subfigure}
77	\hfill
78	\begin{subfigure}[b]{0.48\columnwidth}
79	\centering
80	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig3_operating_regime.pdf}
81	\caption{$C_0$ vs.\ SICA$-$SC gap across all conditions.}
82	\label{fig:operating_regime}
83	\end{subfigure}
84	\caption{Capability trap diagnostics. (a) High vote entropy does not benefit from constraint filtering. (b) The accuracy gap stays centered at zero regardless of answer diversity.}
85	\label{fig:diagnostics}
86	\end{figure}
87	
88	
89	\subsection{Constraint Ablation: MAX-SAT Degenerates to Counting}
90	\label{sec:ablation}
91	
92	If self-extracted constraints are non-discriminative, MAX-SAT should produce the same predictions as baselines ignoring satisfiability. We test three variants on Qwen2.5-14B (FOLIO + ProofWriter, $n = 804$): full SICA, count-only (most constraints wins, ignoring satisfiability), and random-weight (randomized weights before solving).
93	
94	\begin{table}[t]
95	\centering
96	\small
97	\caption{Constraint ablation (Qwen2.5-14B, $n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter). Agreement: predictions identical to SC out of 804.}
98	\label{tab:ablation}
99	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrc@{}}
100	\toprule
101	Aggregation & Acc.\ (\%) & Agreement w/ SC \\
102	\midrule
103	SC (majority vote) & 76.24 & --- \\
104	SICA (MAX-SAT) & 76.49 & 773\,/\,804 \\
105	Count-only & 76.62 & 774\,/\,804 \\
106	Random-weight & 75.87 & 764\,/\,804 \\
107	\bottomrule
108	\end{tabular}
109	\end{table}
110	
111	MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 803 of 804 predictions; the satisfiability solver contributes nothing beyond vote counting (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}). Against SC, all three variants disagree on 30--40 predictions (773--774 for SICA and count-only, 764 for random-weight), but wins and losses cancel: among SICA's 31 discordant pairs with SC, 16 favor SICA, 14 favor SC, and 1 produces different wrong answers, yielding a net gain of only $+$2 problems. Random-weight deviates most from SC (764/804) and achieves the lowest accuracy (75.87\%): randomized constraint weights introduce noise without discriminative benefit. Self-extracted constraints carry no signal beyond vote counts. The oracle control ($+8.82$~pp) confirms that the aggregation mechanism works given independent problem structure; self-extracted constraints simply fail to provide it.
112	
113	
114	\subsection{Targeted Interventions}
115	\label{sec:interventions}
116	
117	The capability trap could stem from prompt design, self-reference circularity, or model-generation limitations. We test three interventions, each targeting a distinct hypothesized cause. Contrastive extraction forces the model to generate answer-discriminative constraints by presenting all candidate answers in the prompt. Cross-model extraction uses Mistral-7B to extract constraints from Qwen2.5-14B traces, breaking the self-reference loop. Generational comparison evaluates Qwen3-14B to test whether next-generation capabilities resolve the trap. All three interventions fail to produce statistically significant improvement over SC (Table~\ref{tab:interventions}).
118	
119	\begin{table}[t]
120	\centering
121	\small
122	\caption{Targeted interventions on FOLIO ($n{=}204$ except $^\dagger$: $n{=}182$, extraction failures excluded). None reaches significance ($\alpha = 0.05$).}
123	\label{tab:interventions}
124	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrl@{}}
125	\toprule
126	Condition & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
127	\midrule
128	Qwen2.5-14B (baseline) & 75.00 & 74.51 & $-$0.49 & n.s. \\
129	\;\; + Contrastive$^\dagger$ & 73.63 & 72.53 & $-$1.10 & 0.752 \\
130	\;\; + Cross-model & 75.00 & 75.00 & 0.00 & --- \\
131	Qwen3-14B (generational) & 83.82 & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 0.655 \\
132	\bottomrule
133	\end{tabular}
134	\end{table}
135	
136	\paragraph{Contrastive Extraction.}
137	Rather than extracting constraints from individual traces, contrastive extraction presents all candidate answers and explicitly requests constraints that distinguish between them. On the 182 FOLIO problems with successful extraction, contrastive-SICA achieves 72.53\% versus SC 73.63\% ($\Delta = -1.10$\,pp, $p = 0.752$). The discriminative rate is 6.0\%, below the baseline 8.2\%. Despite explicit instructions to generate answer-discriminative constraints, the model produces constraints that remain coupled to its preferred answer: 43.2\% supporting and 56.8\% opposing constraints distribute along the same answer bias present in the original traces.
138	
139	\paragraph{Generational Comparison: Qwen3-14B.}
140	Qwen3-14B in non-thinking mode tests whether next-generation model capabilities break the trap. SC improves from 75.00\% (Qwen2.5-14B) to 83.82\%, an $+8.82$\,pp generational gain in base reasoning. The SICA gap shifts from $-0.49$\,pp to $+0.49$\,pp ($p = 0.655$), with only 5 discordant pairs out of 204 (3 SICA wins, 2 SC wins, 97.5\% agreement). Extraction rate improves marginally from 98.5\% to 99.5\%. Generational capability gains lift both SC and SICA equally without producing discriminative constraints.
141	
142	\paragraph{Cross-Model Extraction.}
143	Cross-model extraction uses Mistral-7B-Instruct to extract constraints from Qwen2.5-14B traces, removing the self-reference loop where the same model generates and formalizes reasoning. Cross-model SICA produces identical predictions to SC on all 204 problems ($\Delta = 0.00$\,pp), with a discriminative rate of 0\%. The trace structure, not model identity, determines constraint content: a different model extracting from the same traces produces equally non-discriminative constraints.
144

The problem is extraction vacuity: constraints are syntactically valid but semantically redundant with their source answers. Even the highest-quality condition (LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO, extraction rate 0.94) yields $\\Delta = -2.45$~pp. A temperature ablation ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\\}$, Qwen2.5-14B, $n{=}60$) shows $C_0$ increasing monotonically from 30.0\\% ($T{=}0.3$) to 38.3\\% ($T{=}0.5$), 40.0\\% ($T{=}0.7$), and 43.3\\% ($T{=}1.0$), yet $\\Delta$ never exceeds zero: 0.0~pp at $T{=}0.3$, 0.0~pp at $T{=}0.5$, $-3.33$~pp at $T{=}0.7$ ($n{=}60$, n.s.), and 0.0~pp at $T{=}1.0$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:diagnostics}a). Even at the default temperature $T{=}0.7$, SICA does not outperform SC.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\begin{subfigure}[b]{0.48\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig2_capability_scatter.pdf}\n\\caption{Vote entropy vs.\\ constraint exclusion rate (Qwen2.5-14B, $n{=}307$ multi-candidate problems).}\n\\label{fig:capability_scatter}\n\\end{subfigure}\n\\hfill\n\\begin{subfigure}[b]{0.48\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig3_operating_regime.pdf}\n\\caption{$C_0$ vs.\\ SICA$-$SC gap across all conditions.}\n\\label{fig:operating_regime}\n\\end{subfigure}\n\\caption{Capability trap diagnostics. (a) High vote entropy does not benefit from constraint filtering. (b) The accuracy gap stays centered at zero regardless of answer diversity.}\n\\label{fig:diagnostics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Constraint Ablation: MAX-SAT Degenerates to Counting}\n\\label{sec:ablation}\n\nIf self-extracted constraints are non-discriminative, MAX-SAT should produce the same predictions as baselines ignoring satisfiability. We test three variants on Qwen2.5-14B (FOLIO + ProofWriter, $n = 804$): full SICA, count-only (most constraints wins, ignoring satisfiability), and random-weight (randomized weights before solving).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Constraint ablation (Qwen2.5-14B, $n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter). Agreement: predictions identical to SC out of 804.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & Acc.\\ (\\%) & Agreement w/ SC \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC (majority vote) & 76.24 & --- \\\\\nSICA (MAX-SAT) & 76.49 & 773\\,/\\,804 \\\\\nCount-only & 76.62 & 774\\,/\\,804 \\\\\nRandom-weight & 75.87 & 764\\,/\\,804 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMAX-SAT and count-only agree on 803 of 804 predictions; the satisfiability solver contributes nothing beyond vote counting (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}). Against SC, all three variants disagree on 30--40 predictions (773--774 for SICA and count-only, 764 for random-weight), but wins and losses cancel: among SICA's 31 discordant pairs with SC, 16 favor SICA, 14 favor SC, and 1 produces different wrong answers, yielding a net gain of only $+$2 problems. Random-weight deviates most from SC (764/804) and achieves the lowest accuracy (75.87\\%): randomized constraint weights introduce noise without discriminative benefit. Self-extracted constraints carry no signal beyond vote counts. The oracle control ($+8.82$~pp) confirms that the aggregation mechanism works given independent problem structure; self-extracted constraints simply fail to provide it.\n\n\n\\subsection{Targeted Interventions}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nThe capability trap could stem from prompt design, self-reference circularity, or model-generation limitations. We test three interventions, each targeting a distinct hypothesized cause. Contrastive extraction forces the model to generate answer-discriminative constraints by presenting all candidate answers in the prompt. Cross-model extraction uses Mistral-7B to extract constraints from Qwen2.5-14B traces, breaking the self-reference loop. Generational comparison evaluates Qwen3-14B to test whether next-generation capabilities resolve the trap. All three interventions fail to produce statistically significant improvement over SC (Table~\\ref{tab:interventions}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Targeted interventions on FOLIO ($n{=}204$ except $^\\dagger$: $n{=}182$, extraction failures excluded). None reaches significance ($\\alpha = 0.05$).}\n\\label{tab:interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nCondition & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-14B (baseline) & 75.00 & 74.51 & $-$0.49 & n.s. \\\\\n\\;\\; + Contrastive$^\\dagger$ & 73.63 & 72.53 & $-$1.10 & 0.752 \\\\\n\\;\\; + Cross-model & 75.00 & 75.00 & 0.00 & --- \\\\\nQwen3-14B (generational) & 83.82 & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 0.655 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Contrastive Extraction.}\nRather than extracting constraints from individual traces, contrastive extraction presents all candidate answers and explicitly requests constraints that distinguish between them. On the 182 FOLIO problems with successful extraction, contrastive-SICA achieves 72.53\\% versus SC 73.63\\% ($\\Delta = -1.10$\\,pp, $p = 0.752$). The discriminative rate is 6.0\\%, below the baseline 8.2\\%. Despite explicit instructions to generate answer-discriminative constraints, the model produces constraints that remain coupled to its preferred answer: 43.2\\% supporting and 56.8\\% opposing constraints distribute along the same answer bias present in the original traces.\n\n\\paragraph{Generational Comparison: Qwen3-14B.}\nQwen3-14B in non-thinking mode tests whether next-generation model capabilities break the trap. SC improves from 75.00\\% (Qwen2.5-14B) to 83.82\\%, an $+8.82$\\,pp generational gain in base reasoning. The SICA gap shifts from $-0.49$\\,pp to $+0.49$\\,pp ($p = 0.655$), with only 5 discordant pairs out of 204 (3 SICA wins, 2 SC wins, 97.5\\% agreement). Extraction rate improves marginally from 98.5\\% to 99.5\\%. Generational capability gains lift both SC and SICA equally without producing discriminative constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-Model Extraction.}\nCross-model extraction uses Mistral-7B-Instruct to extract constraints from Qwen2.5-14B traces, removing the self-reference loop where the same model generates and formalizes reasoning. Cross-model SICA produces identical predictions to SC on all 204 problems ($\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp), with a discriminative rate of 0\\%. The trace structure, not model identity, determines constraint content: a different model extracting from the same traces produces equally non-discriminative constraints.\n",

    }

```text
1	# Paper Framing — symb_invariant_consensus
2	
3	## Target Venue
4	- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25)
5	- Deadline: May 25, 2026 (ARR submission)
6	
7	## Core Claims (4 条) — D052 Regime-Dependent Capability Trap (REVISED)
8	
9	1. **The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap**: LLM self-extracted FOL constraint aggregation faces a regime-dependent tension. When model accuracy is high (SC ≥ ~70%), self-extracted constraints correlate strongly with the model's own answers (confirmation bias), losing discriminative power and reducing MAX-SAT to counting. When model accuracy is low (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, enabling SICA to provide genuine gains. The trap is not universal but accuracy-conditioned. Validated across 3 reasoning domains × 5 model families spanning instruction-scale, distilled-reasoning, and full-reasoning architectures. [Scope: this finding characterizes same-model self-extraction pipelines; methods using external verifiers or trained extractors may not share this limitation.]
10	2. **Regime Boundary — SC Accuracy Determines SICA Effectiveness**: (a) **High-accuracy regime (trap active)**: Qwen-14B SC=75.49% Δ=+0.25pp n.s. (n=804), Qwen-14B LogiQA SC=78.8% Δ=0.0pp (n=151 interim), LLaMA-8B StrategyQA Δ=+2.0pp n.s. (n=200), LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204), DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B Δ=-3.3pp (n=60). Full reasoning model QwQ-32B C0<5% (degenerate). (b) **Low-accuracy regime (trap partially lifted)**: Mistral-7B SC=54.41% Δ=**+4.90pp p=0.013** (n=204, b=12 c=2; McNemar confirmed by programmatic computation, three manual attempts all had transcription errors). BUT: LLaMA-8B FOLIO Δ=-2.4pp (n=204) — need LLaMA-8B SC to determine if this is also low-SC (which would complicate the simple accuracy-threshold story) or medium-SC. The boundary may involve more than just SC accuracy (model architecture, constraint extraction quality, variable naming consistency).
11	3. **C0 as Necessary-but-Insufficient Diagnostic**: C0 reliably predicts when symbolic consensus CANNOT help (C0<5% → method degenerates to SC), but high C0 does NOT guarantee gains. C0 combined with SC accuracy forms a two-dimensional screening: low C0 → degenerate; high C0 + high SC → trap; high C0 + low SC → potential gains. Zero-cost early stopping criterion for practitioners.
12	4. **Oracle Proves Aggregation Mechanism, Localizes Bottleneck**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT achieves +14.22pp over SC (89.71% vs 75.49%, p=2.0e-7), proving the aggregation paradigm IS effective with discriminative constraints. Even Mistral-7B's significant +4.90pp captures only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality remains the binding bottleneck across all regimes. Six remediation strategies all failed to close this gap (contrastive prompting, cross-model extraction, generational scaling, solver-guided refinement, consistency filtering, cross-trace UNSAT).
13	
14	## Core Insight
15	LLM self-extracted FOL constraints exhibit a **regime-dependent capability trap**: their discriminative power depends on the extracting model's own accuracy on the task. When a model is strong (SC ≥ ~70%), its self-extracted constraints are strongly correlated with its own answers (confirmation bias), losing the independence needed to adjudicate between candidates. MAX-SAT over such constraints degenerates to counting. When a model is weak (SC ≤ ~55%), constraints retain partial independence from final answers, and SICA can provide significant gains (Mistral-7B: +4.90pp, p=0.013, b=12 c=2). However, even in the favorable regime, self-extracted constraints capture only ~35% of the oracle ceiling (+14.22pp), confirming extraction quality as the persistent bottleneck.
16	
17	**Key evidence**:
18	- **Oracle control (exp-031)**: Gold FOL + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown-type questions +31.9pp. Proves aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints.
19	- **Regime boundary**: Qwen-14B (SC=75.49%) Δ=+0.25pp n.s. vs Mistral-7B (SC=54.41%) Δ=+4.90pp p=0.013. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%) Δ=-2.45pp shows the boundary is sharp (not all weak models benefit).
20	- **Within-model regime transition (D053)**: ⚠️ **PENDING VERIFICATION (M021)** — Original data source overwritten. Claimed: ICL Oracle on Mistral-7B boosted SC from 54.4%→61.5%, SICA flipped from +4.90pp to -2.23pp. Rerun deployed on westd-16639. DO NOT cite in paper until verified by new complete run.
21	- **Entropy-conditioned mechanism (constraint precision analysis)**: Per-problem answer entropy analysis reveals the MECHANISM behind the regime boundary. Conditioned on high entropy (>1.0), BOTH Mistral (+9.6pp) and Qwen-14B (+4.8pp) benefit from SICA. The regime difference is the PROPORTION of high-entropy problems: Mistral 35.8% vs Qwen 10.3%. Causal chain: strong model → consistent traces → low entropy → constraints redundant → solver trivial. Quantified: unanimous problems 18.6% (Mistral) vs 58.8% (Qwen); override precision 70.6% vs 28.6%; confirmation bias (>80% constraint agreement) 31.9% vs 62.3%.
22	- **Six remediation failures**: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model extraction (exp-030, 0.00pp), generational scaling (exp-027, +0.49pp n.s.), solver-guided refinement (exp-036, 0.9% UNSAT trigger rate), consistency filtering (constraints too sparse), cross-trace UNSAT (56.4% zero shared variables). All failed to close the extraction-oracle gap, confirming the trap is structural across prompting, architecture, capability, and verification dimensions.
23	
24	We provide the first systematic empirical study (a) identifying the regime boundary where self-extracted constraint aggregation transitions from ineffective to beneficial, (b) localizing the failure mode to extraction quality via oracle control, (c) quantifying constraint non-discrimination at per-problem granularity, and (d) proposing C0 + SC accuracy as a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic. [Positioning: Logic-LM and LINC documented that LLM-to-FOL translation is error-prone; our contribution is the oracle-controlled causal separation of extraction vs aggregation failure, the regime boundary characterization, and systematic quantification across 5 model families.]
25	
26	## Story Spine
27	- **Problem**: Recent work proposes augmenting LLM reasoning with symbolic methods, extracting formal constraints from reasoning traces and using solvers for answer selection. But when should practitioners invest in such methods vs. simple majority voting?
28	- **Existing approaches & gaps**: SC (Wang et al. 2023), USC (Chen et al. 2023), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023), ORM/PRM (Cobbe et al. 2021, Lightman et al. 2023), Weighted SC (Taubenfeld et al. 2024). None systematically characterize when symbolic aggregation helps vs. when it degenerates to voting.
29	- **Our framing**: We instantiate symbolic consensus as SICA (MAX-SAT over extracted FOL constraints) and systematically evaluate across 3 domains × 5 model families + 1 full reasoning model. We discover a regime-dependent capability trap: SICA gains are accuracy-conditioned, not universally absent.
30	- **Headline result**: "Across 5 model families, SICA's effectiveness depends on the extracting model's baseline accuracy. Strong models (SC ≥ 70%) show no gain (Δ = -3.3pp to +2.0pp, all n.s.); a weaker model (Mistral-7B, SC = 54%) shows significant improvement (+4.90pp, p = 0.013). Oracle control (+14.22pp) proves the aggregation mechanism works with discriminative constraints, but even the best self-extraction result captures only ~35% of this ceiling. Six remediation strategies all fail to close the gap. We identify a confirmation-bias mechanism: stronger models' constraints mirror their own answers, losing discriminative power. C0 + SC accuracy form a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic."
31	
32	## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Defensive Experiments
33	1. **"Gains not statistically significant"** → Four powered tests: exp-001 (n=804), exp-019 (n=200), exp-020 (n=204), exp-010 (n=60). Combined N>1,200.
34	2. **"Only a better tiebreaker, not worth the overhead"** → exp-002: K ablation (SICA≤SC all K including odd K=11); exp-013: MaxSAT≈count-only (mechanism vacuous); exp-009: overhead analysis
35	3. **"Only Qwen models"** → 5 model families: Qwen-14B + Mistral-7B + LLaMA-8B + DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B + QwQ-32B. Critically, Mistral-7B shows the ONLY significant positive (+4.90pp, p=0.013), strengthening cross-family coverage AND revealing the regime boundary.
36	4. **"Only one reasoning model"** → QwQ-32B (full reasoning, C0<5%). DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B reclassified as instruction-scale (C0=58%, behaves like instruction model due to 7B capacity insufficient for deterministic reasoning chains)
37	5. **"Only logic domain"** → FOLIO (formal logic) + StrategyQA (commonsense) + ARC-Challenge (science). ProofWriter in appendix.
38	6. **"MAX-SAT is over-engineering"** → exp-013: MaxSAT ≈ count-only ≈ random-weight confirmed. This IS the capability trap evidence.
39	7. **"Random tiebreak baseline"** → exp-003: SICA tiebreak 80% vs random 50% vs alphabetical 60%. On tied questions SICA is better, but ties are too rare to matter at scale.
40	8. **"Why not just use a reasoning model?"** → Full reasoning model QwQ-32B shows C0<5%, SICA redundant. Distilled reasoning model DeepSeek-R1-7B behaves like instruction model (C0=58%). Either way SICA provides no benefit.
41	9. **"How does this compare to Logic-LM / LINC?"** → exp-015: Logic-LM × K baseline (CPU-only)
42	10. **"Weighted SC is simpler"** → exp-016: Weighted SC baseline (CPU-only)
43	11. **"C0 is trivially derived"** → C0 is trivial to compute but non-trivial to interpret. Its value is as a zero-cost early stopping criterion, not as a metric per se.
44	12. **"Within-family scaling?"** → exp-004 (7B) + exp-007 (72B-AWQ) if time permits. C0 trend within Qwen family.
45	13. **"Self-extraction confound — same model generates and extracts"** → exp-031 Oracle FOLIO (post-fix traces): **RESOLVED.** Gold FOL + Z3 + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC = 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown +31.9pp. Aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints. Bottleneck conclusively localized to LLM self-extraction quality. Three remediation attempts all failed: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model Mistral→Qwen (exp-030, 0pp), Qwen3 generational (exp-027, +0.49pp p=0.655 n.s.). This is now a core contribution, not just a defensive experiment.
46	14. **"Published methods achieve gains on same benchmarks (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer)"** → Acknowledge in Related Work and Discussion. Key distinction: those methods use trained/external verifiers, multi-paradigm solver selection, or logic-complete decomposition — all fundamentally different from same-model self-extraction. Our finding characterizes the specific failure mode of self-extraction pipelines, not symbolic reasoning broadly.
47	15. **"MAX-SAT formulation is just weighted voting"** → Paper must explicitly describe cross-answer evaluation: constraints from trace t_i ARE/ARE NOT evaluated against other candidates. If structurally a weighted vote, acknowledge and frame contribution as "even the most charitable interpretation (weighted vote with constraint-based weights) provides no gain."
48	
49	## Working Title
50	**"When Does Symbolic Aggregation Help? The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap of Self-Extracted Constraints"**
51	(Alternatives: 
52	- "The Capability Trap: Self-Extracted Constraints Help Weak Models but Not Strong Ones"
53	- "Diversity Without Discrimination: A Systematic Study of When LLM-Extracted Constraint Aggregation Succeeds and Fails")
54	
55	NOTE (Dream D037): Title MUST be scoped to "self-extracted constraints" / "SICA" — NOT "symbolic consensus" broadly. Published methods (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer, LogicAgent) achieve gains on same benchmarks using different symbolic approaches. Our evidence covers one pipeline (same-model FOL extraction + MAX-SAT), not the entire paradigm.
56	
57	## Paper Structure (EMNLP 8pp + appendix)
58	- Main Tables: T1 (Main Results: models × domains, SICA vs SC, highlighting regime boundary), T2 (Oracle Control: Gold-FOL+MAX-SAT vs SC vs SICA-self, causal separation), T3 (Constraint Ablation: MaxSAT vs count-only vs random for high-SC model), T4 (Remediation Attempts: 6 interventions all failed to close oracle gap), T5 (Entropy-Conditioned Analysis: SICA Δ by entropy bin × model, showing SICA works for BOTH models on high-entropy problems)
59	- Main Figures: F1 (Method Pipeline), F2 (SC Accuracy vs SICA Δ scatter: hero figure showing regime boundary, x=SC%, y=Δpp, with significance markers), F3 (Entropy-conditioned SICA Δ: bar chart showing Mistral vs Qwen across entropy bins — the mechanism figure), F4 (Confirmation bias quantification: unanimous rate, override precision, constraint agreement)
60	- Appendix: K ablation (exp-002), Temperature ablation (C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime), Per-domain detailed results, Overhead analysis (exp-009), Case studies (exp-008), QwQ-32B/DeepSeek-R1 negative control data, 6 remediation strategy details
61	
62	## Claim Risk Notes (Updated D052 — regime-dependent framing)
63	- **McNemar RESOLVED**: p=0.013 confirmed (b=12, c=2, programmatic computation). Three manual attempts all had transcription errors.
64	- **Claim 1 regime boundary — RESOLVED (D055)**: Sharp boundary at ~55% SC. Within-model ICL transition (D053) is decisive: same Mistral-7B, SC 54%→61.5%, SICA flips from +4.90pp→-2.23pp. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%, Δ=-2.45pp) is ABOVE the boundary, consistent with sharp threshold. All SC>60% conditions show Δ∈[-2.45, +0.49] n.s. The boundary is sharp, not gradual.
65	- **Claim 2 confirmation bias mechanism**: Hypothesis needs per-problem precision analysis comparing Mistral-7B vs Qwen-14B. OPEN. Also: Mistral contradiction rate 3.5% (128/204 problems with contradictions) vs Qwen ~1.5% — higher contradiction rate = more MaxSAT operating room in low-SC regime. **Variable naming inconsistency** (exp-036b): 56.4% of cross-trace answer pairs have zero shared Z3 variables, preventing meaningful cross-trace comparison. This is a key mechanism of the capability trap — even when constraints exist, inconsistent variable naming makes them incomparable across traces.
66	- **Claim 3 two-dimensional diagnostic**: C0 × SC theoretically motivated but empirically thin. Temperature ablation confirms C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime. Need low-SC C0 variation data. OPEN.
67	- **Claim 4 oracle gap**: Mistral +4.90pp vs Oracle +14.22pp = ~35% recovery. Compelling evidence extraction quality is bottleneck even when trap partially lifted.
68	- **exp-011 +10pp is noise**: n=30 SE≈9pp. exp-020 same task n=204 shows -2.4pp. Do NOT cite.
69	- **F2 hero figure redesign**: SC accuracy (x) vs SICA Δ (y) scatter with all model-domain conditions. Need exp-038 LogiQA + exp-035 Qwen-7B SC for more data points. OPEN.
70	- **RESOLVED (D055) — regime boundary is accuracy-dependent, not model-specific**: Within-model ICL transition (D053) controls for model architecture: same Mistral-7B, only SC changes (54%→61.5%), SICA flips sign. LLaMA-8B SC=63.2% is above boundary, consistent. Framing: sharp threshold at ~55% SC, below which constraints retain partial independence. No fallback needed.
71	- **Reviewer contradictions assessed (all dismissed)**:
72	  - Mistral D012 (SC=48.3%, Δ=0): n=60, only 4 discordant pairs (b=2, c=2). Massively underpowered. Paper scoping rule: n=60 is "suggestive" not "confirming."
73	  - LLaMA FOLIO (SC=63.2%, Δ=-2.45pp): Already resolved — above sharp boundary at ~55%.
74	  - LLaMA StrategyQA (SC=72%, Δ=+2.0pp): Not significant (p=0.42, b=9, c=5). Noise.
75	  - Entropy analysis provides the deeper mechanism: SC is a proxy for trace diversity (entropy). The real determinant is the proportion of high-entropy problems, which correlates with but is not identical to SC.
76	
77	## Required Citations (Dream D037 — minimum 30 references)
78	
79	Paper Agent MUST include these categories (non-exhaustive list of must-cite works):
80	- **SC & extensions**: Wang et al. 2023 (SC), Chen et al. 2023 (USC), Loo et al. 2025 (SC scaling plateaus), Taubenfeld et al. 2025 ACL Findings (confidence-weighted SC), DiVeRSe (Li et al. 2023)
81	- **Neuro-symbolic reasoning (positive results on same benchmarks)**: DyLoS (EACL 2026), Aristotle (ACL 2025), FoVer (TACL 2025), LogicAgent (2025), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023)
82	- **Test-time compute scaling**: Snell et al. 2025 (ICLR Oral), DeepSeek-R1 technical report (Nature 2025), Diversity-Aware Training for TTS (2025)
83	- **Verification & reranking**: ORM (Cobbe et al. 2021), PRM (Lightman et al. 2023), Math-Shepherd (Wang et al. 2024)
84	- **Constraint satisfaction in NLP**: SATBench (EMNLP 2025), LLM Meets Constraint Propagation (IJCAI 2025)
85	- **Negative results in NLP**: appropriate precedents for analysis/negative-result papers
86	- **Debate & multi-agent**: Liang et al. 2023, Du et al. 2023
87	- **Reasoning model paradigm**: QwQ technical blog, o1 (OpenAI 2024)
88	
89	## Claim Scoping Rules (Dream D037)
90	
91	- n=60 conditions: present as "suggestive/consistent with" NOT "confirming." Only n≥200 conditions can "confirm" null.
92	- Combined N>1,300: acceptable if clearly stated that 804/1300 come from powered Qwen-14B condition.
93	- Domain coverage: acknowledge that SICA operates meaningfully (C0>20%) primarily on formal logic; other domains are degenerate (C0<10%) due to model capability.
94	- "Capability trap" framing: position as systematic quantification of a known challenge (not discovery). Our specific contributions: (a) per-problem precision analysis at n=307 granularity, (b) constraint ablation proving MAX-SAT ≈ counting, (c) C0 as deployment diagnostic.
95	
96	## Venue Fit Risks
97	- EMNLP: Analysis paper with both positive and negative results. Stronger venue fit than pure negative: characterizes WHEN symbolic aggregation helps (practitioner value). "So what should practitioners do?" → C0 + SC accuracy two-dimensional diagnostic + overhead analysis.
98	- Cross-discipline: MAX-SAT needs clear explanation for NLP audience.
99	- Statistical power: Strong (N>1,200 combined; Mistral positive n=204 with p=0.013).
100	- Novelty upgrade: Regime-dependent finding is more publishable than "it never works" — provides actionable boundary condition.
101

Strong models (SC ≥ 70%) show no gain (Δ = -3.3pp to +2.0pp, all n.s.); a weaker model (Mistral-7B, SC = 54%) shows significant improvement (+4.90pp, p = 0.013). Oracle control (+14.22pp) proves the aggregation mechanism works with discriminative constraints, but even the best self-extraction result captures only ~35% of this ceiling. Six remediation strategies all fail to close the gap. We identify a confirmation-bias mechanism: stronger models' constraints mirror their own answers, losing discriminative power. C0 + SC accuracy form a two-dimensional deployment diagnostic.\"\n\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Defensive Experiments\n1. **\"Gains not statistically significant\"** → Four powered tests: exp-001 (n=804), exp-019 (n=200), exp-020 (n=204), exp-010 (n=60). Combined N>1,200.\n2. **\"Only a better tiebreaker, not worth the overhead\"** → exp-002: K ablation (SICA≤SC all K including odd K=11); exp-013: MaxSAT≈count-only (mechanism vacuous); exp-009: overhead analysis\n3. **\"Only Qwen models\"** → 5 model families: Qwen-14B + Mistral-7B + LLaMA-8B + DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B + QwQ-32B. Critically, Mistral-7B shows the ONLY significant positive (+4.90pp, p=0.013), strengthening cross-family coverage AND revealing the regime boundary.\n4. **\"Only one reasoning model\"** → QwQ-32B (full reasoning, C0<5%). DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B reclassified as instruction-scale (C0=58%, behaves like instruction model due to 7B capacity insufficient for deterministic reasoning chains)\n5. **\"Only logic domain\"** → FOLIO (formal logic) + StrategyQA (commonsense) + ARC-Challenge (science). ProofWriter in appendix.\n6. **\"MAX-SAT is over-engineering\"** → exp-013: MaxSAT ≈ count-only ≈ random-weight confirmed. This IS the capability trap evidence.\n7. **\"Random tiebreak baseline\"** → exp-003: SICA tiebreak 80% vs random 50% vs alphabetical 60%. On tied questions SICA is better, but ties are too rare to matter at scale.\n8. **\"Why not just use a reasoning model?\"** → Full reasoning model QwQ-32B shows C0<5%, SICA redundant. Distilled reasoning model DeepSeek-R1-7B behaves like instruction model (C0=58%). Either way SICA provides no benefit.\n9. **\"How does this compare to Logic-LM / LINC?\"** → exp-015: Logic-LM × K baseline (CPU-only)\n10. **\"Weighted SC is simpler\"** → exp-016: Weighted SC baseline (CPU-only)\n11. **\"C0 is trivially derived\"** → C0 is trivial to compute but non-trivial to interpret. Its value is as a zero-cost early stopping criterion, not as a metric per se.\n12. **\"Within-family scaling?\"** → exp-004 (7B) + exp-007 (72B-AWQ) if time permits. C0 trend within Qwen family.\n13. **\"Self-extraction confound — same model generates and extracts\"** → exp-031 Oracle FOLIO (post-fix traces): **RESOLVED.** Gold FOL + Z3 + MAX-SAT = 89.71% vs SC = 75.49% (+14.22pp, p=2.0e-7, b=29 c=0). Unknown +31.9pp. Aggregation mechanism IS effective with discriminative constraints. Bottleneck conclusively localized to LLM self-extraction quality. Three remediation attempts all failed: contrastive prompting (exp-029, -1.1pp), cross-model Mistral→Qwen (exp-030, 0pp), Qwen3 generational (exp-027, +0.49pp p=0.655 n.s.). This is now a core contribution, not just a defensive experiment.\n14. **\"Published methods achieve gains on same benchmarks (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer)\"** → Acknowledge in Related Work and Discussion. Key distinction: those methods use trained/external verifiers, multi-paradigm solver selection, or logic-complete decomposition — all fundamentally different from same-model self-extraction. Our finding characterizes the specific failure mode of self-extraction pipelines, not symbolic reasoning broadly.\n15. **\"MAX-SAT formulation is just weighted voting\"** → Paper must explicitly describe cross-answer evaluation: constraints from trace t_i ARE/ARE NOT evaluated against other candidates. If structurally a weighted vote, acknowledge and frame contribution as \"even the most charitable interpretation (weighted vote with constraint-based weights) provides no gain.\"\n\n## Working Title\n**\"When Does Symbolic Aggregation Help? The Regime-Dependent Capability Trap of Self-Extracted Constraints\"**\n(Alternatives: \n- \"The Capability Trap: Self-Extracted Constraints Help Weak Models but Not Strong Ones\"\n- \"Diversity Without Discrimination: A Systematic Study of When LLM-Extracted Constraint Aggregation Succeeds and Fails\")\n\nNOTE (Dream D037): Title MUST be scoped to \"self-extracted constraints\" / \"SICA\" — NOT \"symbolic consensus\" broadly. Published methods (DyLoS, Aristotle, FoVer, LogicAgent) achieve gains on same benchmarks using different symbolic approaches. Our evidence covers one pipeline (same-model FOL extraction + MAX-SAT), not the entire paradigm.\n\n## Paper Structure (EMNLP 8pp + appendix)\n- Main Tables: T1 (Main Results: models × domains, SICA vs SC, highlighting regime boundary), T2 (Oracle Control: Gold-FOL+MAX-SAT vs SC vs SICA-self, causal separation), T3 (Constraint Ablation: MaxSAT vs count-only vs random for high-SC model), T4 (Remediation Attempts: 6 interventions all failed to close oracle gap), T5 (Entropy-Conditioned Analysis: SICA Δ by entropy bin × model, showing SICA works for BOTH models on high-entropy problems)\n- Main Figures: F1 (Method Pipeline), F2 (SC Accuracy vs SICA Δ scatter: hero figure showing regime boundary, x=SC%, y=Δpp, with significance markers), F3 (Entropy-conditioned SICA Δ: bar chart showing Mistral vs Qwen across entropy bins — the mechanism figure), F4 (Confirmation bias quantification: unanimous rate, override precision, constraint agreement)\n- Appendix: K ablation (exp-002), Temperature ablation (C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime), Per-domain detailed results, Overhead analysis (exp-009), Case studies (exp-008), QwQ-32B/DeepSeek-R1 negative control data, 6 remediation strategy details\n\n## Claim Risk Notes (Updated D052 — regime-dependent framing)\n- **McNemar RESOLVED**: p=0.013 confirmed (b=12, c=2, programmatic computation). Three manual attempts all had transcription errors.\n- **Claim 1 regime boundary — RESOLVED (D055)**: Sharp boundary at ~55% SC. Within-model ICL transition (D053) is decisive: same Mistral-7B, SC 54%→61.5%, SICA flips from +4.90pp→-2.23pp. LLaMA-8B (SC=63.2%, Δ=-2.45pp) is ABOVE the boundary, consistent with sharp threshold. All SC>60% conditions show Δ∈[-2.45, +0.49] n.s. The boundary is sharp, not gradual.\n- **Claim 2 confirmation bias mechanism**: Hypothesis needs per-problem precision analysis comparing Mistral-7B vs Qwen-14B. OPEN. Also: Mistral contradiction rate 3.5% (128/204 problems with contradictions) vs Qwen ~1.5% — higher contradiction rate = more MaxSAT operating room in low-SC regime. **Variable naming inconsistency** (exp-036b): 56.4% of cross-trace answer pairs have zero shared Z3 variables, preventing meaningful cross-trace comparison. This is a key mechanism of the capability trap — even when constraints exist, inconsistent variable naming makes them incomparable across traces.\n- **Claim 3 two-dimensional diagnostic**: C0 × SC theoretically motivated but empirically thin. Temperature ablation confirms C0 varies but Δ flat in high-SC regime. Need low-SC C0 variation data. OPEN.\n- **Claim 4 oracle gap**: Mistral +4.90pp vs Oracle +14.22pp = ~35% recovery. Compelling evidence extraction quality is bottleneck even when trap partially lifted.\n- **exp-011 +10pp is noise**: n=30 SE≈9pp. exp-020 same task n=204 shows -2.4pp. Do NOT cite.\n- **F2 hero figure redesign**: SC accuracy (x) vs SICA Δ (y) scatter with all model-domain conditions. Need exp-038 LogiQA + exp-035 Qwen-7B SC for more data points. OPEN.\n- **RESOLVED (D055) — regime boundary is accuracy-dependent, not model-specific**: Within-model ICL transition (D053) controls for model architecture: same Mistral-7B, only SC changes (54%→61.5%), SICA flips sign. LLaMA-8B SC=63.2% is above boundary, consistent. Framing: sharp threshold at ~55% SC, below which constraints retain partial independence. No fallback needed.\n- **Reviewer contradictions assessed (all dismissed)**:\n  - Mistral D012 (SC=48.3%, Δ=0): n=60, only 4 discordant pairs (b=2, c=2). Massively underpowered. Paper scoping rule: n=60 is \"suggestive\" not \"confirming.\"\n  - LLaMA FOLIO (SC=63.2%, Δ=-2.45pp): Already resolved — above sharp boundary at ~55%.\n  - LLaMA StrategyQA (SC=72%, Δ=+2.0pp): Not significant (p=0.42, b=9, c=5). Noise.\n  - Entropy analysis provides the deeper mechanism: SC is a proxy for trace diversity (entropy). The real determinant is the proportion of high-entropy problems, which correlates with but is not identical to SC.\n\n## Required Citations (Dream D037 — minimum 30 references)\n\nPaper Agent MUST include these categories (non-exhaustive list of must-cite works):\n- **SC & extensions**: Wang et al. 2023 (SC), Chen et al. 2023 (USC), Loo et al. 2025 (SC scaling plateaus), Taubenfeld et al. 2025 ACL Findings (confidence-weighted SC), DiVeRSe (Li et al. 2023)\n- **Neuro-symbolic reasoning (positive results on same benchmarks)**: DyLoS (EACL 2026), Aristotle (ACL 2025), FoVer (TACL 2025), LogicAgent (2025), Logic-LM (Pan et al. 2023), LINC (Olausson et al. 2023)\n- **Test-time compute scaling**: Snell et al. 2025 (ICLR Oral), DeepSeek-R1 technical report (Nature 2025), Diversity-Aware Training for TTS (2025)\n- **Verification & reranking**: ORM (Cobbe et al. 2021), PRM (Lightman et al. 2023), Math-Shepherd (Wang et al. 2024)\n- **Constraint satisfaction in NLP**: SATBench (EMNLP 2025), LLM Meets Constraint Propagation (IJCAI 2025)\n- **Negative results in NLP**: appropriate precedents for analysis/negative-result papers\n- **Debate & multi-agent**: Liang et al. 2023, Du et al. 2023\n- **Reasoning model paradigm**: QwQ technical blog, o1 (OpenAI 2024)\n\n## Claim Scoping Rules (Dream D037)\n\n- n=60 conditions: present as \"suggestive/consistent with\" NOT \"confirming.\" Only n≥200 conditions can \"confirm\" null.\n- Combined N>1,300: acceptable if clearly stated that 804/1300 come from powered Qwen-14B condition.\n- Domain coverage: acknowledge that SICA operates meaningfully (C0>20%) primarily on formal logic; other domains are degenerate (C0<10%) due to model capability.\n- \"Capability trap\" framing: position as systematic quantification of a known challenge (not discovery). Our specific contributions: (a) per-problem precision analysis at n=307 granularity, (b) constraint ablation proving MAX-SAT ≈ counting, (c) C0 as deployment diagnostic.\n\n## Venue Fit Risks\n- EMNLP: Analysis paper with both positive and negative results. Stronger venue fit than pure negative: characterizes WHEN symbolic aggregation helps (practitioner value). \"So what should practitioners do?\" → C0 + SC accuracy two-dimensional diagnostic + overhead analysis.\n- Cross-discipline: MAX-SAT needs clear explanation for NLP audience.\n- Statistical power: Strong (N>1,200 combined; Mistral positive n=204 with p=0.013).\n- Novelty upgrade: Regime-dependent finding is more publishable than \"it never works\" — provides actionable boundary condition.\n",

Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching.
